# V1.3 Map Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Put the engine on screen and keep it current. Build the `level`/`describe`/`search` APIs and the new Map UI: levels, the 50-box cap with "+N more", the detail panel, ⌘K search and the status pill. Add the live channel, and a file watcher with incremental updates (S6 ≤ 2 s per saved file) and debounced summary refresh. Retire the old graph UI and the Phase 2–4 pipeline behind renderer-agnostic contracts (`CONTRACTS_VERSION` 2). Fix V1.2's four deferred items first, and settle S1's thin margin.

**Architecture:** Two processes share one SQLite file (WAL).
- **The Next server is a reader.** Its route handlers call pure engine functions: `level`, `describe`, `search` and `readIndexStatus`. An **index version** (a per-repo counter in `meta`) tells the Map when to refetch.
- **A forked background worker owns every write while the Map is open** (ADR-038). It runs the file watcher, the incremental structure update, summary refreshes, the folder sweep and embeddings.
  - The incremental update re-resolves only what can have changed and writes only the differences: imports, level-edge counts, folder counts.
- **The live channel** has two routes. A localhost-only `POST /api/live` takes tool events. `GET /api/live/stream` fans them out to the open Maps over SSE.
- **The Map** is a React Flow canvas of at most 50 boxes per level, laid out by dagre. It talks to the server only through `app/map/api.ts`.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript 5, Next.js 16.2 (App Router route handlers + `instrumentation.ts`), React 19, React Flow 12.11, better-sqlite3 + Drizzle (SQLite 3.53, FTS5), Vitest 4, tsx 4. **New dependencies:** `@dagrejs/dagre@3.1.1` (layout, ADR-011) and `chokidar@4.0.3` (watcher, PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6).

**Spec:** `docs/PIVOT_SPEC.md`: slice V1.3 (§12), the Map (§7, §7.1), the live channel (§8), incremental updates (§5.6), success criteria S1, S3, S4 and S6 (§2). Decisions ADR-011, ADR-029, ADR-030, ADR-032, ADR-033. This plan adds ADR-036 → ADR-040 and amends ADR-027, ADR-031 (only if Task 23 needs it), ADR-032 and ADR-033. It builds on the V1.1 plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-11-v1-1-engine-foundation.md`, whose fixture ground truth still holds) and the V1.2 plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-11-v1-2-understanding.md`, including its three addenda).

## Global Constraints

- **Branch:** `pivot/v1`. **After every completed step that changes files (each task, each fix, each doc update), commit and push.**
- **Another session may share this checkout.** Commit by path, so nothing another session staged rides along:
  `git add -A -- <paths> && git commit -m "<message>" -- <paths> && git push origin pivot/v1`.
  `-A` also stages the deletions in Task 21.
- **Never stage `docs/LEARNING_NOTES.md` or `lib/agent/state.ts`**: they're the owner's work in progress.
- **Commit messages** use a conventional prefix (`feat:`, `fix:`, `test:`, `refactor:`, `docs:`, `chore:`). **No `Co-Authored-By` line and no Claude attribution of any kind.**
- **Gate before every commit:** `npx tsc --noEmit && npm run lint && npx vitest run`.
  - Lint *warnings* are fine (the `setEdges` warning in `GraphCanvas` goes in Task 21); errors are not.
  - Each task states how many tests it adds. The suite had 326 tests when this plan was written; Task 0 records the real baseline.
- **Test expectations are derived by hand** from the fixture and the ground truth below. Never run the code and paste its output into a test.
- **No network in tests.**
  - Claude goes through the stub `Summarizer` (`test/helpers/summarizer.ts`).
  - Embedding tests pass a fake `EmbedFn`.
  - The watcher test uses the real file system under the OS temp folder.
- **Paths** stored, compared or sent over HTTP are repo-relative with forward slashes. `""` is the repo root.
- **Layering** (ARCHITECTURE.md §3):
  - Nothing under `lib/` imports `app/`, `next` or `react`.
  - **The UI imports nothing from `lib/` except `import type` from `lib/contracts.ts`.** It reaches the server only through `app/map/api.ts`.
  - **All SQL lives in `lib/db/*`.**
- **Parameter cap:** every bulk insert goes through `lib/db/batch.ts`, and every `IN (…)` list is chunked (`chunkRows`).
- **Contracts:** extend `lib/contracts.ts` only together with the `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` section that defines the shape (CLAUDE.md).
- **Next.js 16 differs from older versions** (`AGENTS.md`). Before writing a route handler or `instrumentation.ts`, read:
  - `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/15-route-handlers.md`;
  - `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/instrumentation.md`.
  The code in this plan follows them.
- **Real money:**
  - The only API calls V1.3 makes are "summarize now" requests during UI verification (Tasks 15 and 18): at most 5, under 1¢.
  - The background worker must never send more than `MAX_AUTO_REFRESH_FILES` (50) file summaries per refresh, and stays under the cumulative `DEFAULT_BUDGET_USD` ($15).
- **Preconditions:**
  - V1.2 is finished (Task 12's full summary pass on VS Code done and recorded), so the Map has taglines.
  - No `npm run summarize` is running while the background worker runs against VS Code, and none during any benchmark.
- **Browser checks** run in the desktop app's built-in browser pane:
  - `preview_start` with `{ name: "nebulosity-dev" }` (from `.claude/launch.json`). Never start the dev server through Bash.
  - Prefer `read_page`, `find`, `get_page_text` and `javascript_tool` for facts, and take a screenshot for each visual change.
  - The pane has been unreliable before (memory: `session-working-mode`). If it hangs or shows a stale server, stop and restart the preview once; if it still misbehaves, ask the owner to look and describe what they see.
  - Until Task 17 switches the page, don't open `/` in a browser: the old page's `GET /api/graph` rewrites a repositories row for the Nebulosity checkout. Check routes with `curl` against the running preview instead.
- **Docs change in the same commit as the design they describe** (maintenance rule); decisions get ADRs. Replace each `‹today›` with the commit's date (YYYY-MM-DD) and each `‹…›` measurement with the measured value.

## Review Focus

Five inputs or conditions most likely to hurt a real user that the spec doesn't spell out. Each line names the task whose test pins it.

1. **A folder with hundreds of children.** VS Code has 36 folders over 50 children; one generated folder holds 780 files. The level must draw exactly 50 boxes, the last being "+731 more" with the folded boxes' lines merged into it, and the panel must list what's folded, 20 at a time.
   - Pinned in Task 2 (`foldNodes` on 780 children; `foldEdges` merges and drops lines).
   - Task 22's `bench-map` asserts ≤ 50 boxes on every VS Code folder.
2. **Paths with spaces, `#`, `%`, `+`, non-ASCII, Windows backslashes, a trailing slash, or `..`.** Every route must round-trip the first ones and refuse traversal with a 400, never a 500 or a file outside the repo.
   - Pinned in Task 2 (`parseRepoPath`) and Task 16 (the API client percent-encodes paths).
3. **A burst of file events: a branch switch, a formatter over the whole tree, a `git pull`.**
   - Structure updates must batch: a 1 s quiet spell, but never more than 5 s.
   - More than 500 paths at once falls back to one full run.
   - Automatic summary refresh never sends more than 50 files per window, so a pull can't spend money silently.
   - Pinned in Task 11 (batcher max wait) and Task 12 (> 500 paths re-index; 52 changed files refresh only 50).
4. **A web page open in the user's browser.** It can't make the server spend money (`POST /api/summarize`), spoof UI actions (`POST /api/live`), re-index or switch repos, even through DNS rebinding. The dev server listens on 127.0.0.1 only.
   - Pinned in Task 14 (`isLocalRequest`: foreign Host or Origin refused).
5. **The background worker dies or restarts while the Map waits on it.**
   - A pending "summarize now" or re-index must answer with an error at once, never hang.
   - Requests while it's down fail fast.
   - It restarts with a growing delay and comes back pointed at the same repo.
   - Pinned in Task 13 (supervisor tests with a fake child).

## Ground truth (hand-derived; tests transcribe these)

These extend the V1.1 plan's fixture ground truth (8 files, 11 edges, 6 folders, 10 level edges) and the V1.2 plan's summary ground truth (14 targets; 13 need a model, 1 template).

**The Map's order.** A level's children are its subfolders plus the files directly inside. They're sorted:
1. by file count, descending (a file counts 1);
2. then by how many files import them, descending (folders count 0);
3. then by path in code-unit order.

**Levels of the fixture** (nothing summarized yet: every box `summaryStatus: "pending"`, or `"off"` without a key):

| Level | Boxes, in order (path · kind · fileCount) | Lines (source → target · count), sorted by source then target |

| `""` | `src` · folder · 7; `scripts` · folder · 1 | `scripts → src · 2` |
| `src` | `src/app` · folder · 2; `src/core` · folder · 2; `src/util` · folder · 2; `src/index.ts` · file · 1 | `src/app → src/core · 1`; `src/app → src/util · 1`; `src/index.ts → src/app · 1`; `src/index.ts → src/core · 1`; `src/util → src/core · 1` |

| `src/util` | `src/util/strings.ts` (2); `src/util/strings.test.ts` (0) | `strings.test.ts → strings.ts · 1` |

Breadcrumbs: `""` → `[""]`; `src/app` → `["", "src", "src/app"]`.

**Summaries stage** (stub summaries, all 14 targets current after a full run):
- After editing `src/core/log.ts` and re-indexing, the stage *still stores* `ready 14/14`: that's the bug.
- A refresh corrects it to `idle 13/14, "1 waiting to be summarized"`, and `log.ts`'s row turns `stale`.
- After `summarizeNow("src/core/log.ts")` it's `ready 13/14, "1 out of date (redone when opened)"`: `src/core` is now lazy.

### Task 1: The index version

The Map needs to know when what it shows has changed, from any process. A counter per repo in `meta`, bumped by every write that changes a level or a description, is the cheapest signal: one indexed read. It's a counter, not a timestamp, so two writes in the same millisecond still read as two changes.

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/db/store/version.ts`, `lib/db/store/version.test.ts`
- Modify: `lib/db/store/summaries.ts` (`applyTargetUpdates`, `writeResults`, `pruneSummaries`), `lib/index/pipeline.ts`
- Docs: `docs/DATABASE_DESIGN.md` (a new §9)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `readIndexVersion(db: Db, repoId: number): number`
  - `bumpIndexVersion(db: Db, repoId: number): number`, which returns the new value
  - From now on, every summary write that changes a row bumps the repo's version, and so does the end of every structure run.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `lib/db/store/version.test.ts`:
```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { copyFixture } from "../../../test/helpers/fixture";
import { stubSummarizer } from "../../../test/helpers/summarizer";
import { getDb } from "../client";
import { indexRepository } from "../../index/pipeline";
import { runSummaries } from "../../index/summarize/run";
import { upsertRepository } from "./structure";
import { bumpIndexVersion, readIndexVersion } from "./version";

describe("index version", () => {
    it("is 0 for a repo nothing has written yet, and counts up by one per bump", () => {
        const db = getDb();
        const repoId = upsertRepository(db, "/version-test/counter");
        expect(readIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(0);
        expect(bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(1);
        expect(bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(2);
        expect(readIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(2);
    });

    it("keeps each repo's count apart", () => {
        const db = getDb();
        const a = upsertRepository(db, "/version-test/a");
        const b = upsertRepository(db, "/version-test/b");
        bumpIndexVersion(db, a);
        expect(readIndexVersion(db, b)).toBe(0);
    });

    it("moves once at the end of every structure run", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        const first = readIndexVersion(getDb(), repoId);
        expect(first).toBe(1);
        indexRepository(root);
        expect(readIndexVersion(getDb(), repoId)).toBe(2);
    });

    it("moves when summaries are written, and not when a run finds every summary current", async () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        const db = getDb();

        await runSummaries(root, { summarizer: stubSummarizer().summarizer, transport: "direct" });
        const afterRun = readIndexVersion(db, repoId);
        expect(afterRun).toBeGreaterThan(1);

        // every inputHash still matches: no row is created, changed or answered, so nothing on the Map changed
        await runSummaries(root, { summarizer: stubSummarizer().summarizer, transport: "direct" });
        expect(readIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(afterRun);
    });

    it("moves when a deleted file takes its summary with it", async () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        await runSummaries(root, { summarizer: stubSummarizer().summarizer, transport: "direct" });
        const db = getDb();
        const before = readIndexVersion(db, repoId);

        fs.rmSync(path.join(root, "scripts/build.js"));
        indexRepository(root);
        // pruning build.js's and scripts/'s summaries bumps once; the end of the run bumps again
        expect(readIndexVersion(db, repoId)).toBe(before + 2);
    });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run them to make sure they fail** — `npx vitest run lib/db/store/version.test.ts`. Expected: FAIL with `Failed to resolve import "./version"`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** — `lib/db/store/version.ts`:
```ts
import { eq, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
import type { Db } from "../client";
import { meta } from "../schema";

/**
 * A per-repo counter that goes up whenever anything the Map shows changes: the structure (files, folders, level edges,
 * symbols, imports) or a summary's text or status (PIVOT_SPEC.md §6.1, ADR-036). Readers cache per version and the Map
 * refetches when it moves. It lives in `meta` like the stage rows, so every process sees every other's writes.
 */
const versionKey = (repoId: number): string => `indexVersion:${repoId}`;

export function readIndexVersion(db: Db, repoId: number): number {
    const row = db.select({ value: meta.value }).from(meta).where(eq(meta.key, versionKey(repoId))).get();
    return row ? Number(row.value) : 0;
}

/** Adds one and returns the new value. One statement, so two processes bumping at once can't lose a bump. */
export function bumpIndexVersion(db: Db, repoId: number): number {
    const updatedAt = new Date();
    db.insert(meta)
        .values({ key: versionKey(repoId), value: "1", updatedAt })
        .onConflictDoUpdate({ target: meta.key, set: { value: sql`cast(${meta.value} as integer) + 1`, updatedAt } })
        .run();
    return readIndexVersion(db, repoId);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Bump on summary writes** — in `lib/db/store/summaries.ts`:
  1. Add `import { bumpIndexVersion } from "./version";` after the `../batch` import.
  2. Replace the whole `applyTargetUpdates` function (keep its doc comment) with:
```ts
export function applyTargetUpdates(db: Db, repoId: number, updates: readonly TargetUpdate[]): Map<string, number> {
    const now = new Date();
    const existing = new Set(loadSummaries(db, repoId).map((s) => targetKey(s.kind, s.path)));
    let changed = false;

    db.transaction((tx) => {
        const created = updates
            .filter((u) => !existing.has(targetKey(u.kind, u.path)))
            .map((u) => ({
                repoId,
                targetKind: u.kind,
                targetPath: u.path,
                status: u.status ?? ("pending" as const),
                tagline: u.templateText?.tagline ?? null,
                summary: u.templateText?.summary ?? null,
                model: u.templateText ? "template" : null,
                inputHash: u.templateText ? u.inputHash : null,
                updatedAt: now,
            }));
        forEachBatch(created, columnCount(summaries), (batch) => { tx.insert(summaries).values(batch).run(); });
        if (created.length > 0) changed = true;

        for (const u of updates){
            if (!existing.has(targetKey(u.kind, u.path))) continue;
            const row = and(eq(summaries.repoId, repoId), eq(summaries.targetKind, u.kind), eq(summaries.targetPath, u.path));

            if (u.templateText){
                const result = tx.update(summaries).set({
                    tagline: u.templateText.tagline, summary: u.templateText.summary, model: "template",
                    inputHash: u.inputHash, status: "skipped", error: null, updatedAt: now,
                }).where(row).run();
                if (result.changes > 0) changed = true;
            } else if (u.status !== null){
                if (tx.update(summaries).set({ status: u.status, updatedAt: now }).where(row).run().changes > 0) changed = true;
            }
        }
    });

    // a new row, a template or a new status changes what the Map shows (ADR-036)
    if (changed) bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId);
    return new Map(loadSummaries(db, repoId).map((s) => [targetKey(s.kind, s.path), s.id]));
}
```
  3. Replace the whole `writeResults` function (keep its doc comment) with:
```ts
export function writeResults(db: Db, results: readonly SummaryResult[]): number {
    const now = new Date();
    const repos = new Set<number>();
    let written = 0;

    db.transaction((tx) => {
        for (const r of results){
            const owner = r.batchId === null ? eq(summaries.id, r.id) : and(eq(summaries.id, r.id), eq(summaries.batchId, r.batchId));
            const answer = r.outcome.ok
                ? { tagline: r.outcome.text.tagline, summary: r.outcome.text.summary, model: r.outcome.model, inputHash: r.inputHash, status: "fresh" as const, error: null }
                : { status: "failed" as const, error: r.outcome.error };

            // Only a regular-API failure keeps the row's claim: a regular-API success supersedes the batch,
            // and a batch's own answer, either way, is the end of that batch's request.
            const releasesClaim = r.outcome.ok || r.batchId !== null;
            const updated = tx.update(summaries)
                .set({ ...answer, ...(releasesClaim ? { batchId: null, pendingHash: null } : {}), updatedAt: now })
                .where(owner).returning({ repoId: summaries.repoId }).all();
            if (updated.length > 0 && r.outcome.ok) tx.delete(summaryVectors).where(eq(summaryVectors.summaryId, r.id)).run();
            for (const row of updated) repos.add(row.repoId);
            written += updated.length;
        }
    });

    // results carry row ids, not repos; the rows they changed say which repos' Maps moved (ADR-036)
    for (const repoId of repos) bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId);
    return written;
}
```
  4. In `pruneSummaries`, replace the body with:
```ts
    const deleted = db.run(sql`
        delete from summaries where repo_id = ${repoId} and (
            (target_kind = 'file' and not exists (select 1 from files f where f.repo_id = ${repoId} and f.path = summaries.target_path))
            or (target_kind = 'folder' and not exists (select 1 from folders d where d.repo_id = ${repoId} and d.path = summaries.target_path))
        )`).changes;
    if (deleted > 0) bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId);
    return deleted;
```

- **The index version** is a `meta` row per repo, `indexVersion:<repoId>`: a counter bumped by one statement whenever anything the Map shows changes. That covers the end of a structure run, an incremental update, and any summary write that creates, rewrites or prunes a row or changes its status. ### Task 2: Map levels

One level of the folder tree, folded to 50 boxes, plus the lines between them. The level edges are precomputed (§4.2), so a level costs three indexed lookups:
- the children;
- their summaries;
- the level's rows of `level_edges`.

That took 0.25 ms on VS Code's busiest level.

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/db/store/map.ts`, `lib/engine/level.ts`, `lib/engine/level.test.ts`
- Modify: `lib/contracts.ts`, `lib/index/paths.ts`, `lib/index/paths.test.ts`
- Docs: `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (§11, plus the banner), `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-036), `docs/PIVOT_SPEC.md` (§7, §7.1)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `readIndexVersion` (Task 1).
- Produces:
  - Contracts: `SummaryStatus`, `MapNodeKind`, `MapNode`, `MapEdge`, `LevelResponse`.
  - `parseRepoPath(raw: string | null | undefined): string | null`
  - In `lib/db/store/map.ts`:
    - types `FolderInfo`, `ChildFile`, `SummaryState`;
    - `loadFolderInfo`, `loadChildFolders`, `loadChildFiles`, `loadChildSummaries`, `loadSummaryState`, `loadLevelEdgeRows`, `loadFileInfo`;
    - helpers (not exported) `inside(column, folder)` and `directlyInside(column, folder)`.
  - In `lib/engine/level.ts`:

    - `level(repoRoot: string, folderPath: string, options?: MapReadOptions): LevelResponse`.

        expect(nodes).toHaveLength(50);
        expect(hidden.size).toBe(0);

        expect(nodes).toHaveLength(50);
        expect(nodes[48].path).toBe("big/f048.ts");
        expect(nodes[49]).toEqual({ path: "big/", name: "+2 more", kind: "more", fileCount: 2, summaryStatus: "skipped", hiddenCount: 2 });
        expect([...hidden]).toEqual(["big/f049.ts", "big/f050.ts"]);
    });

    it("folds VS Code's biggest folder, 780 files, to 50 boxes ending in '+731 more'", () => {
        const { nodes } = foldNodes(files(780), "big");
        expect(nodes).toHaveLength(50);
        expect(nodes[49]).toMatchObject({ name: "+731 more", fileCount: 731, hiddenCount: 731 });
    });

    it("adds up the files of folded folders, and calls the root's '+N more' box '/'", () => {
        const children: MapNode[] = [
            { path: "a", name: "a", kind: "folder", fileCount: 9, summaryStatus: "fresh" },
            { path: "b", name: "b", kind: "folder", fileCount: 4, summaryStatus: "fresh" },
            { path: "c", name: "c", kind: "folder", fileCount: 3, summaryStatus: "fresh" },
        ];
        expect(foldNodes(children, "", 2).nodes[1]).toEqual({ path: "/", name: "+2 more", kind: "more", fileCount: 7, summaryStatus: "skipped", hiddenCount: 2 });
    });
});

describe("foldEdges", () => {
    it("points folded ends at '+N more', adds up lines that now join the same boxes, and drops lines inside it", () => {
        const edges: MapEdge[] = [
            { source: "lvl/a", target: "lvl/x", count: 3 },
            { source: "lvl/a", target: "lvl/y", count: 1 },
            { source: "lvl/y", target: "lvl/a", count: 2 },
            { source: "lvl/x", target: "lvl/y", count: 5 },
            { source: "lvl/b", target: "lvl/c", count: 1 },
        ];
        // "lvl/" sorts before "lvl/a": a prefix comes first
        expect(foldEdges(edges, new Set(["lvl/x", "lvl/y"]), "lvl/")).toEqual([
            { source: "lvl/", target: "lvl/a", count: 2 },
            { source: "lvl/a", target: "lvl/", count: 4 },
            { source: "lvl/b", target: "lvl/c", count: 1 },
        ]);
    });

    it("leaves the lines as they are when nothing is folded", () => {
        const edges: MapEdge[] = [{ source: "b", target: "a", count: 2 }];
        expect(foldEdges(edges, new Set(), "/")).toEqual([{ source: "b", target: "a", count: 2 }]);
    });
});

describe("level helpers", () => {
    it("lists the folders from the root down", () => {
        expect(breadcrumbOf("")).toEqual([""]);
        expect(breadcrumbOf("src/vs/base")).toEqual(["", "src", "src/vs", "src/vs/base"]);
    });

    it("reports text's own status, 'off' only when there's no text, and pending without a row", () => {
        expect(reportedStatus({ tagline: "T", status: "stale" }, true)).toBe("stale");
        expect(reportedStatus({ tagline: null, status: "failed" }, true)).toBe("off");
        expect(reportedStatus({ tagline: null, status: "failed" }, false)).toBe("failed");
        expect(reportedStatus(null, false)).toBe("pending");
        expect(reportedStatus(undefined, true)).toBe("off");
    });
});

- [ ] **Step 4: `parseRepoPath`** — append to `lib/index/paths.ts`:
```ts

/**
 * A repo-relative path from outside (a query string, a tool argument, a live event), checked and put in the spelling
 * stored paths use: forward slashes, no leading "./", no trailing "/", no empty or "." segments. "" is the repo root.
 * Null when it could name a place outside the repo: a ".." segment, an absolute or drive-letter path, a NUL byte.
 */
export function parseRepoPath(raw: string | null | undefined): string | null {
    if (raw === null || raw === undefined) return null;
    const trimmed = normalizeSlashes(raw.trim());
    if (trimmed.includes("\0") || trimmed.startsWith("/") || /^[a-zA-Z]:/.test(trimmed)) return null;

    const segments = trimmed.split("/").filter((segment) => segment !== "" && segment !== ".");
    if (segments.includes("..")) return null;
    return segments.join("/");
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The contract types** — in `lib/contracts.ts`, after the "Index status" block (before "Error envelope"), add:
```ts
/* ─────────────────────────────── Map ─────────────────────────────── */

/**
 * A summary as the UI reports it: the stored statuses (fresh, stale, pending, failed, skipped) plus "off", which is
 * never stored: there's no API key, so a summary that doesn't exist yet can't be written.
 */
export type SummaryStatus = "fresh" | "stale" | "pending" | "failed" | "skipped" | "off";

export type MapNodeKind = "folder" | "file" | "more";

/** One box on the Map — docs/API_CONTRACTS.md §11 */
export type MapNode = {
  /** Repo-relative. The "+N more" box uses its level's path plus "/" ("/" at the root), which no real path can be. */
  path: string;
  /** "workbench", "lifecycle.ts" or "+12 more". */
  name: string;
  kind: MapNodeKind;
  /** Source files inside: 1 for a file; for "+N more", the folded boxes' total. */
  fileCount: number;
  tagline?: string;
  /** "skipped" for the "+N more" box, which has no summary of its own. */
  summaryStatus: SummaryStatus;
  /** "+N more" only: how many boxes it stands for. */
  hiddenCount?: number;
};

/** Imports from one box to another at one level, each import counted once (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2). */
export type MapEdge = { source: string; target: string; count: number };

/** GET /api/level — docs/API_CONTRACTS.md §11 */
export type LevelResponse = {
  /** The repo's root: absolute, forward slashes. */
  repo: string;
  /** The folder on screen; "" is the repo root. */
  path: string;
  /** Folder paths from the root down to `path`: ["", "src", "src/vs"]. */
  breadcrumb: string[];
  /** At most 50 boxes, largest first; "+N more", if any, last. */
  nodes: MapNode[];
  edges: MapEdge[];
  /** How many children "+N more" stands for (0 when everything fits). */
  hiddenCount: number;
  /** Moves whenever anything the Map shows changes: refetch when it does. */
  indexVersion: number;
};

```

- [ ] **Step 6: The Map's queries** — `lib/db/store/map.ts`:
```ts
import { and, eq, sql, type SQL } from "drizzle-orm";
import type { SQLiteColumn } from "drizzle-orm/sqlite-core";
import type { Db } from "../client";
import { files, folders, imports, levelEdges, summaries, type StoredSummaryStatus, type SummaryTargetKind } from "../schema";

/** A folder as the Map draws it. */
export type FolderInfo = { path: string; name: string; fileCount: number };

/** A file directly inside a folder, with how many files import it (its importance, which orders the boxes). */
export type ChildFile = { path: string; lineCount: number; importedBy: number };

/** What the Map shows of one target's summary. */
export type SummaryState = {
    kind: SummaryTargetKind;
    path: string;
    tagline: string | null;
    summary: string | null;
    status: StoredSummaryStatus;
    error: string | null;
};

/**
 * `column` holds a path strictly inside `folder` ("" means anywhere in the repo). It's a range, so SQLite answers it
 * from the (repo, path) index: every path that starts with "src/" sorts at or after "src/" and before "src0", because
 * "0" is the character right after "/".
 */
function inside(column: SQLiteColumn, folder: string): SQL {
    return folder === "" ? sql`1 = 1` : sql`${column} >= ${`${folder}/`} and ${column} < ${`${folder}0`}`;
}

/** `column` holds a path directly inside `folder`: inside it, with no "/" after the folder's own prefix. */
function directlyInside(column: SQLiteColumn, folder: string): SQL {
    const skip = folder === "" ? 0 : folder.length + 1;
    return sql`${inside(column, folder)} and instr(substr(${column}, ${skip + 1}), '/') = 0`;
}

const summaryColumns = {
    kind: summaries.targetKind,
    path: summaries.targetPath,
    tagline: summaries.tagline,
    summary: summaries.summary,
    status: summaries.status,
    error: summaries.error,
};

export function loadFolderInfo(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string): FolderInfo | null {
    return db.select({ path: folders.path, name: folders.name, fileCount: folders.fileCount }).from(folders)
        .where(and(eq(folders.repoId, repoId), eq(folders.path, folderPath))).get() ?? null;
}

/** A folder's subfolders, in path order. */
export function loadChildFolders(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string): FolderInfo[] {
    return db.select({ path: folders.path, name: folders.name, fileCount: folders.fileCount }).from(folders)
        .where(and(eq(folders.repoId, repoId), eq(folders.parentPath, folderPath))).orderBy(folders.path).all();
}

/** The files directly inside a folder, each with how many files import it, in path order. */
export function loadChildFiles(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string): ChildFile[] {
    return db.select({ path: files.path, lineCount: files.lineCount, importedBy: sql<number>`count(${imports.id})` })
        .from(files)
        .leftJoin(imports, eq(imports.targetFileId, files.id))
        .where(and(eq(files.repoId, repoId), directlyInside(files.path, folderPath)))
        .groupBy(files.id)
        .orderBy(files.path)
        .all();
}

/** The summaries of a folder's direct children: its files and subfolders (never the repo's own). */
export function loadChildSummaries(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string): SummaryState[] {
    return db.select(summaryColumns).from(summaries)
        .where(and(
            eq(summaries.repoId, repoId),
            sql`${summaries.targetKind} in ('file', 'folder')`,
            directlyInside(summaries.targetPath, folderPath),
        ))
        .all();
}

export function loadSummaryState(db: Db, repoId: number, kind: SummaryTargetKind, targetPath: string): SummaryState | null {
    return db.select(summaryColumns).from(summaries)
        .where(and(eq(summaries.repoId, repoId), eq(summaries.targetKind, kind), eq(summaries.targetPath, targetPath)))
        .get() ?? null;
}

/** The Map's lines at one level (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2), in source-then-target order: one indexed lookup. */
export function loadLevelEdgeRows(db: Db, repoId: number, levelPath: string): { source: string; target: string; count: number }[] {
    return db.select({ source: levelEdges.sourceNode, target: levelEdges.targetNode, count: levelEdges.count }).from(levelEdges)
        .where(and(eq(levelEdges.repoId, repoId), eq(levelEdges.levelPath, levelPath)))
        .orderBy(levelEdges.sourceNode, levelEdges.targetNode)
        .all();
}

export function loadFileInfo(db: Db, repoId: number, filePath: string): { path: string; lineCount: number; isTest: boolean } | null {
    return db.select({ path: files.path, lineCount: files.lineCount, isTest: files.isTest }).from(files)
        .where(and(eq(files.repoId, repoId), eq(files.path, filePath))).get() ?? null;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: The level** — `lib/engine/level.ts`:
```ts
import path from "path";
import type { LevelResponse, MapEdge, MapNode, SummaryStatus } from "../contracts";
import { getDb, type Db } from "../db/client";
import type { StoredSummaryStatus } from "../db/schema";
import {
    loadChildFiles, loadChildFolders, loadChildSummaries, loadFileInfo, loadFolderInfo, loadLevelEdgeRows,
} from "../db/store/map";
import { requireRepository } from "../db/store/structure";
import { readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { repoKey } from "../index/paths";

/** No more than this many boxes are ever on screen, the "+N more" box included (PIVOT_SPEC.md §2, S4). */
export const MAP_BOX_CAP = 50;

export class PathNotFoundError extends Error {
    constructor(targetPath: string){
        super(`Nothing is indexed at "${targetPath}".`);
        this.name = "PathNotFoundError";
    }
}

/** Thrown by level() for a file: the Map opens folders, and a file is selected inside its folder's level. */
export class NotAFolderError extends Error {
    constructor(filePath: string){
        const folder = filePath.includes("/") ? filePath.slice(0, filePath.lastIndexOf("/")) : "";
        super(`"${filePath}" is a file. Open its folder ("${folder}") and select it there.`);
        this.name = "NotAFolderError";
    }
}

export type MapReadOptions = {
    /** No API key: a summary that doesn't exist yet reads "off", because nothing can write it. */
    summariesOff?: boolean;
};

/**
 * The status the UI shows for one summary row, or for none. Text on show keeps its stored status (fresh, stale,
 * skipped, or failed with older text still shown). With no text, it's "off" when summaries can't run; otherwise it's
 * whatever is stored, or "pending" when there's no row yet.
 */
export function reportedStatus(row: { tagline: string | null; status: StoredSummaryStatus } | null | undefined, summariesOff: boolean): SummaryStatus {
    if (row?.tagline) return row.status;
    if (summariesOff) return "off";
    return row?.status ?? "pending";
}

/** The "+N more" box's path: its level's path plus "/" ("/" at the root). No stored path ends in "/", so it can't collide. */
export function moreNodePath(levelPath: string): string {
    return `${levelPath}/`;
}

/** The folders from the root down to `folderPath`: "src/vs" → ["", "src", "src/vs"]. */
export function breadcrumbOf(folderPath: string): string[] {
    if (folderPath === "") return [""];
    const segments = folderPath.split("/");
    return ["", ...segments.map((_, i) => segments.slice(0, i + 1).join("/"))];
}

const compare = (a: string, b: string): number => (a < b ? -1 : a > b ? 1 : 0);

/**
 * A folder's children as boxes, in the Map's order (ADR-036): its subfolders and the files directly inside, largest
 * first, so the cap folds the smallest (PIVOT_SPEC.md §7). A file counts as 1. Among equals the more-imported file
 * comes first, then the path decides. describe() lists a folder's children in this same order.
 */
export function loadMapChildren(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string, summariesOff: boolean): MapNode[] {
    const states = new Map(loadChildSummaries(db, repoId, folderPath).map((s) => [`${s.kind}:${s.path}`, s]));
    const importedBy = new Map<string, number>();

    const box = (kind: "folder" | "file", nodePath: string, name: string, fileCount: number): MapNode => {
        const state = states.get(`${kind}:${nodePath}`);
        return {
            path: nodePath,
            name,
            kind,
            fileCount,
            ...(state?.tagline ? { tagline: state.tagline } : {}),
            summaryStatus: reportedStatus(state, summariesOff),
        };
    };

    const boxes = [
        ...loadChildFolders(db, repoId, folderPath).map((f) => box("folder", f.path, f.name, f.fileCount)),
        ...loadChildFiles(db, repoId, folderPath).map((f) => {
            importedBy.set(f.path, f.importedBy);
            return box("file", f.path, f.path.slice(f.path.lastIndexOf("/") + 1), 1);
        }),
    ];

    return boxes.sort((a, b) =>
        b.fileCount - a.fileCount || (importedBy.get(b.path) ?? 0) - (importedBy.get(a.path) ?? 0) || compare(a.path, b.path));
}

/**
 * Folds the overflow (PIVOT_SPEC.md §7). With more than `cap` children, the first cap − 1 stay as boxes and the rest
 * become one "+N more" box, so exactly `cap` boxes are drawn. The children come in the Map's order, largest first.
 */
export function foldNodes(children: readonly MapNode[], levelPath: string, cap: number = MAP_BOX_CAP): { nodes: MapNode[]; hidden: Set<string> } {
    if (children.length <= cap) return { nodes: [...children], hidden: new Set() };

    const shown = children.slice(0, cap - 1);
    const folded = children.slice(cap - 1);
    const more: MapNode = {
        path: moreNodePath(levelPath),
        name: `+${folded.length} more`,
        kind: "more",
        fileCount: folded.reduce((sum, n) => sum + n.fileCount, 0),
        summaryStatus: "skipped",
        hiddenCount: folded.length,
    };
    return { nodes: [...shown, more], hidden: new Set(folded.map((n) => n.path)) };
}

/**
 * The level's lines once the overflow is folded. A folded end now points at "+N more", lines that end up joining the
 * same two boxes are added up, and a line between two folded children (now inside "+N more") is dropped.
 */
export function foldEdges(edges: readonly MapEdge[], hidden: ReadonlySet<string>, morePath: string): MapEdge[] {
    const merged = new Map<string, MapEdge>();

    for (const edge of edges){
        const source = hidden.has(edge.source) ? morePath : edge.source;
        const target = hidden.has(edge.target) ? morePath : edge.target;
        if (source === target) continue;

        const key = `${source}\0${target}`;
        const existing = merged.get(key);
        if (existing) existing.count += edge.count;
        else merged.set(key, { source, target, count: edge.count });
    }

    return [...merged.values()].sort((a, b) => compare(a.source, b.source) || compare(a.target, b.target));
}

/** GET /api/level (API_CONTRACTS.md §11): one folder of the Map, its boxes folded to the cap, and the lines between them. */
export function level(repoRoot: string, folderPath: string, options: MapReadOptions = {}): LevelResponse {
    const db = getDb();
    const repo = repoKey(path.resolve(repoRoot));
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repo);

    // one read transaction: a structure update committing meanwhile can't hand us half of its rows
    return db.transaction(() => {
        if (!loadFolderInfo(db, repoId, folderPath)){
            if (loadFileInfo(db, repoId, folderPath)) throw new NotAFolderError(folderPath);
            throw new PathNotFoundError(folderPath);
        }

        const { nodes, hidden } = foldNodes(loadMapChildren(db, repoId, folderPath, options.summariesOff ?? false), folderPath);
        return {
            repo,
            path: folderPath,
            breadcrumb: breadcrumbOf(folderPath),
            nodes,
            edges: foldEdges(loadLevelEdgeRows(db, repoId, folderPath), hidden, moreNodePath(folderPath)),
            hiddenCount: hidden.size,
            indexVersion: readIndexVersion(db, repoId),
        };
    });
}
```

     Then append:
```md

## 11. GET /api/level?path= (slice V1.3)

One folder of the Map (`lib/engine/level.ts`). `path` is repo-relative; empty or missing means the repo root.

```ts
type SummaryStatus = "fresh" | "stale" | "pending" | "failed" | "skipped" | "off";
type MapNode = { path: string; name: string; kind: "folder" | "file" | "more"; fileCount: number;
  tagline?: string; summaryStatus: SummaryStatus; hiddenCount?: number };
type MapEdge = { source: string; target: string; count: number };
type LevelResponse = { repo: string; path: string; breadcrumb: string[]; nodes: MapNode[]; edges: MapEdge[];
  hiddenCount: number; indexVersion: number };
```

- **Boxes** are the folder's subfolders and the files directly inside it, largest first. A file counts as 1; among equals the more-imported file comes first, then the path decides.
- **The cap.** With more than 50 children, the first 49 are drawn and the rest fold into one `kind: "more"` box named `+N more`. Its `path` is the level's path plus `/` (`/` at the root), which no stored path can be. Its `fileCount` adds up the folded boxes', `hiddenCount` is N, and its `summaryStatus` is `skipped`, since it has no summary of its own. So `nodes` never holds more than 50 (S4).
- **Lines** are the level's rows of `level_edges` (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2): each import is counted once, at the deepest folder holding both files. A folded end points at the `+N more` box, lines that then join the same two boxes are added up, and lines between two folded children are dropped.
- **`summaryStatus`** is the stored status when the box has text. Without text it's `off` when there's no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, otherwise the stored status, or `pending` when no row exists yet.
- **`breadcrumb`** lists the folder paths from the root down to `path`.
- **`indexVersion`** moves whenever anything the Map shows changes (ADR-036). A client caches levels per version and refetches when `GET /api/status` reports a new one.

**Errors:**
- `400 BAD_REQUEST`: a path that could leave the repo (`..`, absolute, a drive letter), or a file's path (the Map opens folders).
- `404 NOT_FOUND`: nothing indexed at `path`.
- `404 INDEX_EMPTY`: no active repo yet.
```
  2. **`docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md`.** Append after ADR-035:
```md

## ADR-036 — The Map reads one level at a time: the fold, its order, and an index version (‹today›)

**Context:** ADR-029 fixed the Map's shape: levels, a 50-box cap with "+N more", and precomputed level edges. Building it raised four questions: which boxes fold, what the "+N more" box is called, how a client knows its copy is old, and how "uses / used by" (§4.2) is computed.
**Decision:**
- **Order and fold.** A level's children are sorted by file count (a file counts 1), then by importer count, then by path. Past 50, the first 49 stay and the rest fold into one box, so exactly 50 are drawn. The "+N more" box's path is the level's path plus `/` (`/` at the root): no stored path ends in a slash, so the id can't collide. Lines into folded children are redirected to it and added up; lines between two folded children are dropped.
- **An index version.** A counter per repo in `meta` (`indexVersion:<repoId>`) is bumped by one SQL statement by every write the Map can see: a structure run, an incremental update, and any summary write that changes a row. Level and describe answers carry it, and `GET /api/status` reports it, so a client caches per version and refetches when it moves, and a server cache keyed on it can't serve stale data.
- **Areas from memory.** "Uses / used by" needs every import across a boundary: about 65,000 for `src/vs/workbench`. They're counted from an in-memory import graph (files sorted by path, so a folder is one contiguous range; imports as pairs of positions), loaded once per index version (about 60 ms on VS Code) and cached per path.
**Alternatives:**
- (a) Fold alphabetically: a 780-file folder would hide its most-imported files behind "+731 more".
- (b) A timestamp version: two writes in the same millisecond look like one.
- (c) Area counts in SQL: a join over 139,580 imports per click, about 3 times slower than the in-memory scan and not cacheable across paths.
**Consequences:** A level costs three indexed lookups (0.25 ms for VS Code's busiest level's lines). The first describe after a change pays for loading the graph. A client that polls the version sees changes from any process: the CLI, the background worker, or the MCP server.
**Revisit when:** users want a different order (for example, recently changed first, with E3).
```

### Task 3: Area counts from the import graph

"Uses" and "used by" (§4.2) count the imports that cross a target's boundary, each grouped by the area on the other side. That's the same rule `importerLine` uses in the summary prompt (ADR-034's amendment), and it reuses `lcaFolder` and `childToward` from `lib/index/aggregate.ts`.

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/engine/graph.ts`, `lib/engine/graph.test.ts`
- Modify: `lib/db/store/map.ts`, `lib/contracts.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `readIndexVersion` (Task 1).
- Produces:
  - Contract: `AreaCount = { path: string; kind: "folder" | "file"; count: number }`.
  - Store: `loadGraphFiles(db, repoId): { id: number; path: string }[]` and `loadGraphEdges(db, repoId): { source: number; target: number }[]`.
  - In `lib/engine/graph.ts`:
    - `type ImportGraph = { paths: string[]; sources: Int32Array; targets: Int32Array }`;
    - `type TargetKind = "repo" | "folder" | "file"`;
    - `buildImportGraph(files, edges): ImportGraph`;
    - `rangeOf(graph, targetPath, kind): [number, number]`;
    - `areaCounts(graph, targetPath, kind): { uses: AreaCount[]; usedBy: AreaCount[] }`;
    - `cachedAreaCounts(db, repoId, version, targetPath, kind)`, with the same return type.

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { copyFixture } from "../../test/helpers/fixture";
import { getDb } from "../db/client";
import { loadGraphEdges, loadGraphFiles } from "../db/store/map";
import { readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { indexRepository } from "../index/pipeline";
import { areaCounts, buildImportGraph, cachedAreaCounts, rangeOf } from "./graph";

function fixtureGraph(){
    const { repoId } = indexRepository(copyFixture());
    return buildImportGraph(loadGraphFiles(getDb(), repoId), loadGraphEdges(getDb(), repoId));
}

describe("import graph", () => {
    it("finds a folder's files as one range of the sorted paths, and a file as one position", () => {
        const graph = buildImportGraph(
            [{ id: 7, path: "b/z.ts" }, { id: 3, path: "ab.ts" }, { id: 9, path: "a/y.ts" }, { id: 1, path: "a/x.ts" }],
            [],
        );
        expect(graph.paths).toEqual(["a/x.ts", "a/y.ts", "ab.ts", "b/z.ts"]);
        expect(rangeOf(graph, "a", "folder")).toEqual([0, 2]);   // "ab.ts" is beside a/, not inside it
        expect(rangeOf(graph, "ab.ts", "file")).toEqual([2, 3]);
        expect(rangeOf(graph, "", "repo")).toEqual([0, 4]);
        expect(rangeOf(graph, "nope.ts", "file")).toEqual([3, 3]);
    });

    it("counts a folder's crossing imports by area: src/core is used by src/app, src/index.ts and src/util, and src by scripts", () => {
        const graph = fixtureGraph();
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "src/core", "folder")).toEqual({
            uses: [],
            usedBy: [
                { path: "src/app", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/index.ts", kind: "file", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/util", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
            ],
        });
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "src", "folder")).toEqual({ uses: [], usedBy: [{ path: "scripts", kind: "folder", count: 2 }] });
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "", "repo")).toEqual({ uses: [], usedBy: [] });
    });

    it("counts a file's imports both ways, a neighbour in its own folder being its own area", () => {
        const graph = fixtureGraph();
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "src/app/main.ts", "file")).toEqual({
            uses: [
                { path: "src/app/view.tsx", kind: "file", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/core", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/util", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
            ],
            usedBy: [
                { path: "scripts", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/app/view.tsx", kind: "file", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/index.ts", kind: "file", count: 1 },
            ],
        });
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "scripts/build.js", "file")).toEqual({ uses: [{ path: "src", kind: "folder", count: 2 }], usedBy: [] });
    });

    it("groups a workbench-like folder's imports under the sibling areas they reach, most first", () => {
        const files = [
            "src/vs/workbench/a.ts", "src/vs/workbench/b/c.ts", "src/vs/base/common/x.ts", "src/vs/base/browser/y.ts",
            "src/vs/platform/p.ts", "src/vs/nls.ts", "extensions/e.ts",
        ].map((p, i) => ({ id: i + 1, path: p }));
        const id = (p: string) => files.find((f) => f.path === p)!.id;
        const edge = (s: string, t: string) => ({ source: id(s), target: id(t) });
        const graph = buildImportGraph(files, [
            edge("src/vs/workbench/a.ts", "src/vs/base/common/x.ts"),
            edge("src/vs/workbench/a.ts", "src/vs/base/browser/y.ts"),
            edge("src/vs/workbench/b/c.ts", "src/vs/base/common/x.ts"),
            edge("src/vs/workbench/b/c.ts", "src/vs/platform/p.ts"),
            edge("src/vs/workbench/b/c.ts", "src/vs/nls.ts"),
            edge("src/vs/workbench/b/c.ts", "src/vs/workbench/a.ts"),   // inside: not counted
            edge("extensions/e.ts", "src/vs/workbench/a.ts"),
        ]);
        expect(areaCounts(graph, "src/vs/workbench", "folder")).toEqual({
            uses: [
                { path: "src/vs/base", kind: "folder", count: 3 },
                { path: "src/vs/nls.ts", kind: "file", count: 1 },
                { path: "src/vs/platform", kind: "folder", count: 1 },
            ],
            usedBy: [{ path: "extensions", kind: "folder", count: 1 }],
        });
    });

    it("reuses the graph within an index version and reloads it when the version moves", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        const db = getDb();
        const before = cachedAreaCounts(db, repoId, readIndexVersion(db, repoId), "src/core", "folder");
        expect(before.usedBy.find((a) => a.path === "src/util")?.count).toBe(1);

        // strings.ts now imports log.ts directly as well: a second import from src/util into src/core
        const strings = path.join(root, "src/util/strings.ts");
        fs.writeFileSync(strings, `import { banner } from "../core/log";\n${fs.readFileSync(strings, "utf8")}`);
        indexRepository(root);
        const after = cachedAreaCounts(db, repoId, readIndexVersion(db, repoId), "src/core", "folder");
        expect(after.usedBy.find((a) => a.path === "src/util")?.count).toBe(2);
    });
});

```ts

/** Every file of a repo, by id and path: the import graph's nodes. */
export function loadGraphFiles(db: Db, repoId: number): { id: number; path: string }[] {
    return db.select({ id: files.id, path: files.path }).from(files).where(eq(files.repoId, repoId)).all();
}

/** Every import of a repo as a pair of file ids: the import graph's edges. Imports never cross repos, so the source's repo decides. */
export function loadGraphEdges(db: Db, repoId: number): { source: number; target: number }[] {
    return db.select({ source: imports.sourceFileId, target: imports.targetFileId })
        .from(imports)
        .innerJoin(files, eq(files.id, imports.sourceFileId))
        .where(eq(files.repoId, repoId))
        .all();
}
```

/** Imports across a target's boundary, grouped by the area on the other side (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2). */
export type AreaCount = { path: string; kind: "folder" | "file"; count: number };
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement** — `lib/engine/graph.ts`:
```ts
import type { AreaCount } from "../contracts";
import type { Db } from "../db/client";
import { loadGraphEdges, loadGraphFiles } from "../db/store/map";
import { childToward, lcaFolder } from "../index/aggregate";
import { dirOf } from "../index/paths";

/**
 * A repo's import graph in memory (PIVOT_SPEC.md §6.1). Every file appears once, sorted by path, and every import is a
 * pair of positions in that list. Sorted paths turn "every file inside this folder" into one contiguous range, so asking
 * about a folder is a scan over integer pairs. VS Code's 139,580 imports take about 1 MB here.
 */
export type ImportGraph = { paths: string[]; sources: Int32Array; targets: Int32Array };

/** What a target is, which decides what counts as inside it. */
export type TargetKind = "repo" | "folder" | "file";

type Areas = { uses: AreaCount[]; usedBy: AreaCount[] };

const compare = (a: string, b: string): number => (a < b ? -1 : a > b ? 1 : 0);

export function buildImportGraph(files: readonly { id: number; path: string }[], edges: readonly { source: number; target: number }[]): ImportGraph {
    const sorted = [...files].sort((a, b) => compare(a.path, b.path));
    const position = new Map(sorted.map((f, i) => [f.id, i]));
    const sources = new Int32Array(edges.length);
    const targets = new Int32Array(edges.length);

    edges.forEach((edge, i) => {
        sources[i] = position.get(edge.source)!;
        targets[i] = position.get(edge.target)!;
    });

    return { paths: sorted.map((f) => f.path), sources, targets };
}

/** The first position whose path is ≥ key: binary search over the sorted paths. */
function lowerBound(paths: readonly string[], key: string): number {
    let lo = 0;
    let hi = paths.length;
    while (lo < hi){
        const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1;
        if (paths[mid] < key) lo = mid + 1;
        else hi = mid;
    }
    return lo;
}

/**
 * The positions [from, to) of the files at or under a target: every file for the repo, one file for a file, and a
 * folder's whole subtree for a folder ("src/" ≤ path < "src0", since "0" comes right after "/").
 */
export function rangeOf(graph: ImportGraph, targetPath: string, kind: TargetKind): [number, number] {
    if (kind === "repo") return [0, graph.paths.length];
    if (kind === "file"){
        const at = lowerBound(graph.paths, targetPath);
        return graph.paths[at] === targetPath ? [at, at + 1] : [at, at];
    }
    return [lowerBound(graph.paths, `${targetPath}/`), lowerBound(graph.paths, `${targetPath}0`)];
}

/**
 * "Uses" and "used by" (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2): the imports that cross the target's boundary, each counted under the area
 * on the other side. That area is the child of the lowest folder holding both ends, on the way to the other file, or
 * that file itself when it sits directly in that folder. Most first, ties by path.
 */
export function areaCounts(graph: ImportGraph, targetPath: string, kind: TargetKind): Areas {
    const [from, to] = rangeOf(graph, targetPath, kind);
    const home = kind === "file" ? dirOf(targetPath) : targetPath;
    // one lowest-common-folder walk per file on the other side, however many imports it shares with the target
    const areaOf = new Map<number, AreaCount>();
    const area = (other: number): AreaCount => {
        let found = areaOf.get(other);
        if (!found){
            const otherPath = graph.paths[other];
            const groupPath = childToward(lcaFolder(home, dirOf(otherPath)), otherPath);
            found = { path: groupPath, kind: groupPath === otherPath ? "file" : "folder", count: 0 };
            areaOf.set(other, found);
        }
        return found;
    };

    const uses = new Map<string, AreaCount>();
    const usedBy = new Map<string, AreaCount>();
    const tally = (into: Map<string, AreaCount>, other: number): void => {
        const { path, kind: areaKind } = area(other);
        const entry = into.get(path) ?? { path, kind: areaKind, count: 0 };
        entry.count++;
        into.set(path, entry);
    };

    for (let i = 0; i < graph.sources.length; i++){
        const source = graph.sources[i];
        const target = graph.targets[i];
        const sourceInside = source >= from && source < to;
        const targetInside = target >= from && target < to;
        if (sourceInside && !targetInside) tally(uses, target);
        else if (targetInside && !sourceInside) tally(usedBy, source);
    }

    const ranked = (counts: Map<string, AreaCount>): AreaCount[] =>
        [...counts.values()].sort((a, b) => b.count - a.count || compare(a.path, b.path));
    return { uses: ranked(uses), usedBy: ranked(usedBy) };
}

/** Area answers kept per repo, for one index version at a time; a new version empties them. */
type Cached = { version: number; graph: ImportGraph; areas: Map<string, Areas> };
const cache = new Map<number, Cached>();
/** A bound on one version's answers, not an LRU: past it the map starts over. */
const MAX_CACHED_AREAS = 2_000;

/**
 * Area counts for describe(), from a graph loaded once per repo and index version (PIVOT_SPEC.md §6.1: "cached per
 * index version"). About 60 ms to load on VS Code. Each answer is cached per path until the version moves.
 */
export function cachedAreaCounts(db: Db, repoId: number, version: number, targetPath: string, kind: TargetKind): Areas {
    let entry = cache.get(repoId);
    if (!entry || entry.version !== version){
        entry = { version, graph: buildImportGraph(loadGraphFiles(db, repoId), loadGraphEdges(db, repoId)), areas: new Map() };
        cache.set(repoId, entry);
    }

    const key = `${kind}:${targetPath}`;
    let areas = entry.areas.get(key);
    if (!areas){
        areas = areaCounts(entry.graph, targetPath, kind);
        if (entry.areas.size >= MAX_CACHED_AREAS) entry.areas.clear();
        entry.areas.set(key, areas);
    }
    return areas;
}
```

/** A declaration in a file: top-level ones and class members (`Logger.info`), with the lines to jump to. */
export type SymbolInfo = { name: string; kind: string; startLine: number; endLine: number; exported: boolean };

/** GET /api/describe — docs/API_CONTRACTS.md §12 */
export type DescribeResponse = {
  /** The repo's root: absolute, forward slashes (for vscode:// links). */
  repo: string;
  path: string;
  kind: "repo" | "folder" | "file";
  tagline?: string;
  summary?: string;
  summaryStatus: SummaryStatus;
  /** Why the last attempt failed, when summaryStatus is "failed". */
  summaryError?: string;
  stats: { fileCount: number; lineCount: number };
  /** Folders and the repo: every child, in the Map's order (so the boxes "+N more" folds are the ones after the first 49). */
  children?: MapNode[];
  /** Files: their declarations, in line order. */
  symbols?: SymbolInfo[];
  /** What the target imports, by area, most first. */
  uses: AreaCount[];
  /** What imports the target, by area, most first. */
  usedBy: AreaCount[];
  /** Files at or under the target whose summary is out of date with their bytes. */
  stalePaths: number;
  indexVersion: number;
};
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The store queries** — in `lib/db/store/map.ts`:
  - change the schema import to `import { files, folders, imports, levelEdges, summaries, symbols, type StoredSummaryStatus, type SummaryTargetKind } from "../schema";`;
  - add `import type { SymbolInfo } from "../../contracts";`;
  - then append:
```ts

/** The lines of every file inside a folder ("" = the whole repo). */
export function sumLinesInside(db: Db, repoId: number, folderPath: string): number {
    return db.select({ lines: sql<number>`coalesce(sum(${files.lineCount}), 0)` }).from(files)
        .where(and(eq(files.repoId, repoId), inside(files.path, folderPath))).get()?.lines ?? 0;
}

/** A file's declarations in line order: top-level ones and class members, members by their qualified name. */
export function loadFileSymbols(db: Db, repoId: number, filePath: string): SymbolInfo[] {
    return db.select({
        name: symbols.qualifiedName,
        kind: symbols.kind,
        startLine: symbols.startLine,
        endLine: symbols.endLine,
        exported: symbols.exported,
    })
        .from(symbols)
        .innerJoin(files, eq(files.id, symbols.fileId))
        .where(and(eq(files.repoId, repoId), eq(files.path, filePath)))
        .orderBy(symbols.startLine, symbols.qualifiedName)
        .all();
}

/** How many files at or under a target have a stale summary: text written for bytes the file no longer has. */
export function countStaleFileSummaries(db: Db, repoId: number, targetPath: string, kind: "repo" | "folder" | "file"): number {
    const where = kind === "file" ? eq(summaries.targetPath, targetPath) : inside(summaries.targetPath, kind === "repo" ? "" : targetPath);
    return db.select({ n: sql<number>`count(*)` }).from(summaries)
        .where(and(eq(summaries.repoId, repoId), eq(summaries.targetKind, "file"), eq(summaries.status, "stale"), where))
        .get()?.n ?? 0;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Freshness** — `lib/engine/freshness.ts`:
```ts
import type { Db } from "../db/client";
import { countStaleFileSummaries } from "../db/store/map";
import { plural } from "../index/summarize/prompts";
import type { TargetKind } from "./graph";

/**
 * Files at or under a target whose summary describes bytes they no longer have (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6, "honest answers").
 * An edited file's summary turns stale when the structure update re-plans the summaries (Task 5), and stays stale until
 * it's redone: 60 quiet seconds later in the background worker, at once when opened.
 */
export function stalePaths(db: Db, repoId: number, targetPath: string, kind: TargetKind): number {
    return countStaleFileSummaries(db, repoId, targetPath, kind);
}

/** The sentence an answer adds when it touches stale paths, or null when it touches none. */
export function freshnessNote(stale: number): string | null {
    if (stale === 0) return null;
    return stale === 1
        ? "1 file here changed since it was summarized, so its summary is out of date until it's refreshed."
        : `${plural(stale, "file")} here changed since they were summarized, so their summaries are out of date until they're refreshed.`;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: describe** — `lib/engine/describe.ts`:
```ts
import path from "path";
import type { DescribeResponse } from "../contracts";
import { getDb } from "../db/client";
import { loadFileInfo, loadFileSymbols, loadFolderInfo, loadSummaryState, sumLinesInside } from "../db/store/map";
import { requireRepository } from "../db/store/structure";
import { readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { repoKey } from "../index/paths";
import { stalePaths } from "./freshness";
import { cachedAreaCounts, type TargetKind } from "./graph";
import { loadMapChildren, PathNotFoundError, reportedStatus, type MapReadOptions } from "./level";

/**
 * GET /api/describe (API_CONTRACTS.md §12): what a folder or file is, what's inside it, what it uses and what uses it
 * (PIVOT_SPEC.md §6.1). The detail panel shows it; V1.4's MCP `describe` tool formats it as text.
 */
export function describe(repoRoot: string, targetPath: string, options: MapReadOptions = {}): DescribeResponse {
    const db = getDb();
    const repo = repoKey(path.resolve(repoRoot));
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repo);
    const summariesOff = options.summariesOff ?? false;

    // one read transaction, as level() does: the answer is one consistent picture
    return db.transaction(() => {
        const folder = loadFolderInfo(db, repoId, targetPath);
        const file = folder ? null : loadFileInfo(db, repoId, targetPath);
        if (!folder && !file) throw new PathNotFoundError(targetPath);

        const kind: TargetKind = folder ? (targetPath === "" ? "repo" : "folder") : "file";
        const version = readIndexVersion(db, repoId);
        const row = loadSummaryState(db, repoId, kind, targetPath);
        const summaryStatus = reportedStatus(row, summariesOff);
        const { uses, usedBy } = cachedAreaCounts(db, repoId, version, targetPath, kind);

        return {
            repo,
            path: targetPath,
            kind,
            ...(row?.tagline ? { tagline: row.tagline, summary: row.summary ?? "" } : {}),
            summaryStatus,
            ...(summaryStatus === "failed" && row?.error ? { summaryError: row.error } : {}),
            stats: folder
                ? { fileCount: folder.fileCount, lineCount: sumLinesInside(db, repoId, targetPath) }
                : { fileCount: 1, lineCount: file!.lineCount },
            ...(folder
                ? { children: loadMapChildren(db, repoId, targetPath, summariesOff) }
                : { symbols: loadFileSymbols(db, repoId, targetPath) }),
            uses,
            usedBy,
            stalePaths: stalePaths(db, repoId, targetPath, kind),
            indexVersion: version,
        };
    });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Document it** — append to `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`:
```md

## 12. GET /api/describe?path= (slice V1.3)

What a folder, file or the repo (`path` empty) is (`lib/engine/describe.ts`). The detail panel shows it, and V1.4's MCP `describe` tool formats it as text.

```ts
type SymbolInfo = { name: string; kind: string; startLine: number; endLine: number; exported: boolean };
type AreaCount = { path: string; kind: "folder" | "file"; count: number };
type DescribeResponse = { repo: string; path: string; kind: "repo" | "folder" | "file";
  tagline?: string; summary?: string; summaryStatus: SummaryStatus; summaryError?: string;
  stats: { fileCount: number; lineCount: number };
  children?: MapNode[]; symbols?: SymbolInfo[];
  uses: AreaCount[]; usedBy: AreaCount[]; stalePaths: number; indexVersion: number };
```

- **`children`** (folders and the repo) lists every child in the Map's order, so the boxes a level folds into "+N more" are exactly those after the first 49. A client shows 20 at a time.
- **`symbols`** (files) are the top-level declarations and class members in line order, members by qualified name (`Logger.info`). The lines are the declaration's own, for jumping to it.
- **`uses` / `usedBy`** count imports across the target's boundary, grouped by the area on the other side (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2), most first. An area is a folder, or a file when the other file sits directly in the lowest folder both share.
- **`summaryStatus`** follows §11's rule. **`summaryError`** says why the last attempt failed, and only appears when the status is `failed`.
- **`stalePaths`** counts files at or under the target whose summary is out of date with their bytes. It's non-zero between a file's edit and the refresh of its summary (`PIVOT_SPEC.md` §5.6).

**Errors:** `400 BAD_REQUEST` for a path that could leave the repo; `404 NOT_FOUND`; `404 INDEX_EMPTY`.
```

### Task 5: Stage statuses that stay current (V1.2 deferred item 1)

**The bug.** A stage row describes the moment it was written. Anything that later changes the data underneath leaves it wrong:
- **Summaries.** After an edit and `npm run index`, the stage still reads `ready 14/14`, and the edited file's summary row still says `fresh`. Nothing re-plans until the next summary run.
- **Embeddings.** After a summary is rewritten (its vector deleted), the stage still counts the old vector.

The Map's status pill would show both lies.

**The fix:**
- **Summaries are re-derived by `refreshSummaryStatus`** whenever something other than a finishing summary run changes what's planned. It plans exactly as a run does, but sends nothing: new rows, statuses and test templates. Then it writes the stage from the same pure `summaryStageStatus` a finishing run now uses.
- **Embeddings counts are read live.** Two cheap counts at read time.

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/index/summarize/snapshot.ts`, `lib/index/summarize/status.ts`, `lib/index/summarize/status.test.ts`
- Modify:
  - `lib/index/summarize/run.ts`
  - `lib/db/store/lock.ts`
  - `lib/db/store/summary-vectors.ts`
  - `lib/engine/status.ts` and `lib/engine/status.test.ts`
  - `lib/contracts.ts`
  - `scripts/index.ts`
- Docs: `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-032 amendment), `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` (§9), `docs/DATABASE_DESIGN.md` (§9)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `type Snapshot`, and `planSnapshot(db, repoId, refreshStaleFolders, failedThisRun): Snapshot`. This is run.ts's old private `snapshot`, moved.
  - `type SummaryStageContext = { summarizerAvailable: boolean; inFlight: number; failed?: number; stopped?: string | null }`
  - `summaryStageStatus(targets: readonly PlannedTarget[], context: SummaryStageContext): StageStatus`
  - `refreshSummaryStatus(repoRoot: string, options: { summarizerAvailable: boolean }): StageStatus | null`, which returns null when a live run holds the summarizing lock
  - `stillAtAnthropic(inFlight: number): string`, moved here from run.ts
  - `isSummaryLockHeld(db, now?): boolean`
  - `countEmbedded(db, repoId): number`
  - `liveEmbeddings(stage: StageStatus, live: { done: number; total: number }): StageStatus`
  - `IndexStatus.indexVersion: number`
- Later tasks call `refreshSummaryStatus` after every structure change (Tasks 7, 12; the CLI here).

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { copyFixture } from "../../../test/helpers/fixture";
import { stubSummarizer } from "../../../test/helpers/summarizer";
import { getDb } from "../../db/client";
import { acquireSummaryLock, releaseSummaryLock } from "../../db/store/lock";
import { readStages, writeStage } from "../../db/store/stages";
import { requireRepository } from "../../db/store/structure";
import { loadSummaries } from "../../db/store/summaries";
import { repoKey } from "../paths";
import { indexRepository } from "../pipeline";
import type { PlannedTarget, TargetState } from "./plan";
import { runSummaries } from "./run";
import { refreshSummaryStatus, summaryStageStatus } from "./status";

let made = 0;
const target = (state: TargetState, kind: PlannedTarget["kind"] = "file"): PlannedTarget =>
    ({ kind, path: `t${made++}`, model: "m", inputHash: "h", state, status: null });

/** The fixture, fully summarized by the stub (every target current), then log.ts edited and re-indexed. */
async function editedAfterSummaries(): Promise<string> {
    const root = copyFixture();
    indexRepository(root);
    await runSummaries(root, { summarizer: stubSummarizer().summarizer, transport: "direct" });
    fs.appendFileSync(path.join(root, "src/core/log.ts"), "export const extra = 1;\n");
    indexRepository(root);
    return root;
}

describe("summaryStageStatus", () => {
    it("reads a plan as ready, idle, off, running or stopped", () => {
        const done = [target("current"), target("template")];
        expect(summaryStageStatus([...done, target("lazy", "folder")], { summarizerAvailable: true, inFlight: 0, failed: 2 }))
            .toEqual({ state: "ready", done: 2, total: 3, note: "2 failed this run (retried next run); 1 out of date (redone when opened)" });
        expect(summaryStageStatus([...done, target("ready")], { summarizerAvailable: true, inFlight: 0 }))
            .toEqual({ state: "idle", done: 2, total: 3, note: "1 waiting to be summarized" });
        expect(summaryStageStatus([...done, target("ready"), target("waiting", "folder")], { summarizerAvailable: false, inFlight: 0 }))
            .toEqual({ state: "off", done: 2, total: 4, note: "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set, so 2 of 4 summaries can't be written" });
        expect(summaryStageStatus([...done, target("in-flight")], { summarizerAvailable: true, inFlight: 1 }))
            .toEqual({ state: "running", done: 2, total: 3, note: "1 request at Anthropic in batches; run npm run summarize to collect them" });
        expect(summaryStageStatus(done, { summarizerAvailable: true, inFlight: 3, stopped: "stopped: over budget" }))
            .toEqual({ state: "running", done: 2, total: 2, note: "stopped: over budget; 3 requests still at Anthropic; run npm run summarize to collect them" });
        expect(summaryStageStatus(done, { summarizerAvailable: true, inFlight: 0, stopped: "stopped: over budget" }))
            .toEqual({ state: "failed", done: 2, total: 2, note: "stopped: over budget" });
    });
});

describe("refreshSummaryStatus", () => {
    it("catches the stage up after an edit: the stored row still says ready 14/14 until the refresh says 1 waits", async () => {
        const root = await editedAfterSummaries();
        const db = getDb();
        const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(root));

        // the bug: nothing re-derived the stage after the structure changed underneath it
        expect(readStages(db, repoId).summaries).toMatchObject({ state: "ready", done: 14, total: 14 });

        expect(refreshSummaryStatus(root, { summarizerAvailable: true })).toEqual({ state: "idle", done: 13, total: 14, note: "1 waiting to be summarized" });
        expect(readStages(db, repoId).summaries).toMatchObject({ state: "idle", done: 13, total: 14 });
        expect(loadSummaries(db, repoId).find((s) => s.path === "src/core/log.ts")?.status).toBe("stale");
    });

    it("without a key writes the test template and says how many summaries can't be written", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        indexRepository(root);
        expect(refreshSummaryStatus(root, { summarizerAvailable: false }))
            .toEqual({ state: "off", done: 1, total: 14, note: "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set, so 13 of 14 summaries can't be written" });
        const db = getDb();
        expect(loadSummaries(db, requireRepository(db, repoKey(root))).find((s) => s.path === "src/util/strings.test.ts"))
            .toMatchObject({ status: "skipped", tagline: "Tests for strings" });
    });

    it("leaves the stage row to a summary run that holds the lock, but still updates the summary rows", async () => {
        const root = await editedAfterSummaries();
        const db = getDb();
        const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(root));
        acquireSummaryLock(db);
        try{
            writeStage(db, repoId, "summaries", { state: "running", done: 3, total: 14, note: "wave 1" });
            expect(refreshSummaryStatus(root, { summarizerAvailable: true })).toBeNull();
            expect(readStages(db, repoId).summaries).toMatchObject({ state: "running", done: 3, total: 14, note: "wave 1" });
            expect(loadSummaries(db, repoId).find((s) => s.path === "src/core/log.ts")?.status).toBe("stale");
        } finally{
            releaseSummaryLock(db);
        }
    });

    it("keeps a failed run's note while its targets still wait", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
