- [ ] **Step 3: Move the planning pass out of run.ts** — `lib/index/summarize/snapshot.ts`:
```ts
import type { Db } from "../../db/client";
import { loadFileFacts, loadFolderFacts, loadSummaries, type FileFacts } from "../../db/store/summaries";
import { planSummaries, type PlannedTarget } from "./plan";
import { SUMMARY_MODELS } from "./prompts";

/** One planning pass: the plan, and the file facts it was made from. */
export type Snapshot = { targets: PlannedTarget[]; files: Map<string, FileFacts> };

/**
 * Plans every target of a repo from what the database holds right now. Summary runs, summarizeNow and the stage
 * refresh (status.ts) all plan through here, so they can't disagree about a target's state.
 */
export function planSnapshot(db: Db, repoId: number, refreshStaleFolders: boolean, failedThisRun: ReadonlySet<string>): Snapshot {
    // One read transaction: a structure run committing meanwhile can't hand us half of its changes.
    // (better-sqlite3 has one connection, so reads through `db` inside the callback belong to it.)
    return db.transaction(() => {
        const files = loadFileFacts(db, repoId);
        const targets = planSummaries(files, loadFolderFacts(db, repoId), loadSummaries(db, repoId), {
            models: SUMMARY_MODELS,
            refreshStaleFolders,
            failedThisRun,
        });
        return { targets, files: new Map(files.map((f) => [f.path, f])) };
    });
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The shared stage logic and the refresh** — `lib/index/summarize/status.ts`:
```ts
import path from "path";
import type { StageState, StageStatus } from "../../contracts";
import { getDb } from "../../db/client";
import { isSummaryLockHeld } from "../../db/store/lock";
import { readStages, writeStage } from "../../db/store/stages";
import { requireRepository } from "../../db/store/structure";
import { applyTargetUpdates, countInFlight } from "../../db/store/summaries";
import { repoKey } from "../paths";
import type { PlannedTarget, TargetState } from "./plan";
import { plural } from "./prompts";
import { planSnapshot } from "./snapshot";

/** What the summaries stage's status depends on besides the plan. */
export type SummaryStageContext = {
    /** An API key is set, so a summarizer exists. */
    summarizerAvailable: boolean;
    /** Requests still at Anthropic in Message Batches. */
    inFlight: number;
    /** Requests that failed during the run that's finishing (runs only). */
    failed?: number;
    /** Why that run stopped early: the circuit breaker or the spend cap (runs only). */
    stopped?: string | null;
};

/** The note for requests a stopped run leaves at Anthropic: they're paid for, and the next run collects them. */
export function stillAtAnthropic(inFlight: number): string {
    return `${plural(inFlight, "request")} still at Anthropic; run npm run summarize to collect them`;
}

/**
 * The summaries stage as one plan sees it (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.7). Pure, and shared by the stage's two writers: a
 * finishing summary run, and refreshSummaryStatus after anything else changed what's planned (ADR-032, amended).
 * - stopped (runs only): running while batches are at Anthropic, failed otherwise.
 * - off: no API key, and targets wait for a model.
 * - running: requests are at Anthropic in batches.
 * - idle: targets wait for a model and nothing is sending them yet (a file edited since it was summarized, say).
 * - ready: nothing waits. Folders left out of date for later ("lazy") and this run's failures go in the note.
 */
export function summaryStageStatus(targets: readonly PlannedTarget[], context: SummaryStageContext): StageStatus {
    const count = (state: TargetState): number => targets.filter((t) => t.state === state).length;
    const done = count("current") + count("template");
    const waiting = count("ready") + count("waiting");
    const notes: string[] = [];
    let state: StageState;

    if (context.stopped){
        state = context.inFlight > 0 ? "running" : "failed";
        notes.push(context.stopped);
        if (context.inFlight > 0) notes.push(stillAtAnthropic(context.inFlight));
    } else if (!context.summarizerAvailable && waiting > 0){
        state = "off";
        notes.push(`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set, so ${waiting} of ${targets.length} summaries can't be written`);
    } else if (context.inFlight > 0){
        state = "running";
        notes.push(`${plural(context.inFlight, "request")} at Anthropic in batches; run npm run summarize to collect them`);
    } else if (waiting > 0){
        state = "idle";
        notes.push(`${waiting} waiting to be summarized`);
    } else{
        state = "ready";
        if (context.failed) notes.push(`${context.failed} failed this run (retried next run)`);
        const lazy = count("lazy");
        if (lazy > 0) notes.push(`${lazy} out of date (redone when opened)`);
    }

    return { state, done, total: targets.length, ...(notes.length > 0 ? { note: notes.join("; ") } : {}) };
}

/**
 * Brings the summaries stage up to date after something other than a summary run changed what it depends on: a
 * structure update, or a summarizeNow (ADR-032, amended in V1.3). It plans like a run that sends nothing. New targets
 * get rows, statuses follow the plan (an edited file's summary turns stale), and test templates are written. While a
 * live summary run holds the lock, that run owns the stage row, so only the rows are updated and this returns null.
 * A failed run's note is kept until its targets stop waiting.
 */
export function refreshSummaryStatus(repoRoot: string, options: { summarizerAvailable: boolean }): StageStatus | null {
    const db = getDb();
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(path.resolve(repoRoot)));
    // between runs, out-of-date folders are lazy: redone when opened, or by the background worker's sweep
    const snap = planSnapshot(db, repoId, false, new Set());
    applyTargetUpdates(db, repoId, snap.targets);
    if (isSummaryLockHeld(db)) return null;

    let status = summaryStageStatus(snap.targets, { summarizerAvailable: options.summarizerAvailable, inFlight: countInFlight(db, repoId) });
    const previous = readStages(db, repoId).summaries;
    if (previous?.state === "failed" && status.state === "idle"){
        status = { ...status, state: "failed", ...(previous.note ? { note: previous.note } : {}) };
    }

    writeStage(db, repoId, "summaries", status);
    return status;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: The lock check** — append to `lib/db/store/lock.ts`:
```ts

/** Whether a live summary run holds the summarizing lock: its row is younger than LOCK_STALE_AFTER_MS (a dead run's is older). */
export function isSummaryLockHeld(db: Db, now: Date = new Date()): boolean {
    const holder = readLock(db, META_KEYS.SUMMARIZING_LOCK);
    if (holder === null) return false;
    const since = Date.parse(holder);
    return !Number.isNaN(since) && now.getTime() - since <= LOCK_STALE_AFTER_MS;
}
```

```ts
/** How many model-written summaries have their vector: the embeddings stage's `done`, counted live. */
export function countEmbedded(db: Db, repoId: number): number {
    return db.select({ n: sql<number>`count(*)` }).from(summaries)
        .innerJoin(summaryVectors, eq(summaryVectors.summaryId, summaries.id))
        .where(embeddable(repoId)).get()?.n ?? 0;
}
```

  3. Replace `lib/engine/status.ts` with:
```ts
import type { IndexStatus, StageName, StageStatus } from "../contracts";
import { getDb } from "../db/client";
import { readStages, type StoredStage } from "../db/store/stages";
import { countInFlight } from "../db/store/summaries";
import { countEmbeddable, countEmbedded } from "../db/store/summary-vectors";
import { findRepository } from "../db/store/structure";
import { readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { repoKey } from "../index/paths";
import { plural } from "../index/summarize/prompts";

/** A running stage writes at least once a minute; five silent minutes means its process died. */
export const RUNNING_STALE_AFTER_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000;

/** GET /api/status: what each stage of one repo is doing, or null if that repo was never indexed. */
export function readIndexStatus(repoRoot: string, now: Date = new Date()): IndexStatus | null {
    const db = getDb();
    const repo = repoKey(repoRoot);
    const repoId = findRepository(db, repo);
    if (repoId === null) return null;

    const stored = readStages(db, repoId);
    const inFlight = () => countInFlight(db, repoId);

    return {
        repo,
        stages: {
            structure: honest("structure", stored.structure, now, inFlight),
            summaries: honest("summaries", stored.summaries, now, inFlight),
            embeddings: liveEmbeddings(honest("embeddings", stored.embeddings, now, inFlight), {
                done: countEmbedded(db, repoId),
                total: countEmbeddable(db, repoId),
            }),
        },
        indexVersion: readIndexVersion(db, repoId),
    };
}

/**
 * The embeddings stage with its counts taken from the tables (ADR-032, amended in V1.3): a rewritten summary loses its
 * vector at once, and a stored count would claim it for as long as nothing re-embeds. A running or failed stage keeps
 * its state and note. Otherwise the counts decide: nothing to embed is idle, everything embedded is ready, and anything
 * missing is idle with a note.
 */
export function liveEmbeddings(stage: StageStatus, live: { done: number; total: number }): StageStatus {
    if (stage.state === "running" || stage.state === "failed") return { ...stage, done: live.done, total: live.total };
    if (live.total === 0) return { state: "idle", done: 0, total: 0 };
    if (live.done >= live.total) return { state: "ready", done: live.done, total: live.total };

    const missing = live.total - live.done;
    return { state: "idle", done: live.done, total: live.total, note: `${plural(missing, "summary", "summaries")} without a vector yet` };
}

/**
 * What a stored row means now: a "running" row nobody has written for minutes is a crash,
 * not progress (ADR-032). The exception is summaries with batches still at Anthropic: that
 * work goes on without any local process, so it stays running.
 */
function honest(name: StageName, stage: StoredStage | undefined, now: Date, inFlight: () => number): StageStatus {
    if (!stage) return { state: "idle", done: 0, total: 0 };

    const { updatedAt, ...status } = stage;
    const silent = status.state === "running" && now.getTime() - updatedAt.getTime() > RUNNING_STALE_AFTER_MS;
    if (!silent || (name === "summaries" && inFlight() > 0)) return status;

    return { ...status, state: "failed", note: `interrupted: no progress since ${updatedAt.toISOString()}` };
}
```

### Task 6: A vector always describes its summary's current text (V1.2 deferred item 2)

**The bug.** A search vector can outlive the text it was computed from, in two ways:
- **Rewritten while being embedded.** `embedSummaries` loads a summary's text, embeds it (seconds of model time), then stores the vector. If the text is rewritten in between, the old text's vector lands on the new text. In V1.3's worker, a "summarize now" can land during an embedding slice. Nothing re-embeds a row that has a vector, so search ranks it by text it no longer has, for good.
- **Replaced by a template.** Writing a template over model-written text (`applyTargetUpdates`' template branch) never deleted the old vector. Since V1.2's 11f, a folder can turn test-only and get a template.

**The fix:**
- A vector is stored only if its row still has the `inputHash` it was computed for. Text changes exactly when `inputHash` changes.
- A template write deletes the row's vector.

**Files:**
- Modify: `lib/db/store/summaries.ts`, `lib/db/store/summary-vectors.ts`, `lib/index/embed.ts`
- Test: `lib/db/store/summaries.test.ts`, `lib/index/embed.test.ts`
- Docs: `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-027 amendment), `docs/DATABASE_DESIGN.md` (§9)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `SummaryToEmbed` gains `inputHash: string`.
  - `writeSummaryVectors(db, rows: readonly { summaryId: number; inputHash: string; embedding: Buffer }[]): number` returns how many vectors it stored.
  - `EmbedRunOptions.limit?: number`.
  - `embedSummaries(...)` now returns `{ embedded, total, remaining }`. The background worker (Task 12) embeds in slices with `limit`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Vectors land only on unchanged text** — in `lib/db/store/summary-vectors.ts`:

  2. In `loadSummariesWithoutVectors`:

  3. Replace `writeSummaryVectors` with:
```ts
/**
 * Stores vectors, each only if its summary still has the text it was computed from: the same inputHash, which changes
 * exactly when the text does. The text can be rewritten while it's being embedded (a "summarize now" in the background
 * worker, say), and a vector of the old text would then sit on the new text for good, since nothing re-embeds a row
 * that has one (ADR-027, amended in V1.3). Returns how many it stored.
 */
export function writeSummaryVectors(db: Db, rows: readonly { summaryId: number; inputHash: string; embedding: Buffer }[]): number {
    let written = 0;

    // immediate: take the write lock before reading the hashes, so no other process rewrites a row between the check and the insert
    db.transaction((tx) => {
        for (const row of rows){
            const current = tx.select({ inputHash: summaries.inputHash }).from(summaries).where(eq(summaries.id, row.summaryId)).get();
            if (current?.inputHash !== row.inputHash) continue;
            // onConflictDoNothing: a second embedder racing this one must not crash on summary_id's uniqueness
            written += tx.insert(summaryVectors).values({ summaryId: row.summaryId, embedding: row.embedding }).onConflictDoNothing().run().changes;
        }
    }, { behavior: "immediate" });

    return written;
}
```

```ts

```
  2. Replace `embedSummaries` with:
```ts
export async function embedSummaries(repoRoot: string, options: EmbedRunOptions = {}): Promise<{ embedded: number; total: number; remaining: number }> {
    const db = getDb();
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(path.resolve(repoRoot)));
    const embed = options.embed ?? ((texts: string[]) => embedTexts(texts));
    const groupSize = options.groupSize ?? 256;
    const missing = loadSummariesWithoutVectors(db, repoId);
    const pending = options.limit === undefined ? missing : missing.slice(0, options.limit);
    const total = countEmbeddable(db, repoId);
    let done = total - missing.length;
    let embedded = 0;

    try{
        for (let i = 0; i < pending.length; i += groupSize){
            writeStage(db, repoId, "embeddings", { state: "running", done, total });
            const group = pending.slice(i, i + groupSize);
            const vectors = await embed(group.map(summaryEmbeddingText));
            // a summary rewritten meanwhile is skipped here and picked up, with its new text, next time
            const written = writeSummaryVectors(db, group.map((s, j) => ({ summaryId: s.id, inputHash: s.inputHash, embedding: encodeVector(vectors[j]) })));
            embedded += written;
            done += written;
            options.onProgress?.(done, total);
        }
    } catch (err){
        writeStage(db, repoId, "embeddings", { state: "failed", done, total, note: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
        throw err;
    }

    // the stage's counts are read live (ADR-032, amended), so "ready" here can't hide what a slice left for later
    writeStage(db, repoId, "embeddings", { state: "ready", done, total });
    return { embedded, total, remaining: missing.length - embedded };
}
```

### Task 7: "Summarize now" can't pay twice (V1.2 deferred item 3)

**The bug.** The Map asks for a summary whenever someone opens a path that has none (§5.3). A double click, a re-render or a second tab sends a second request before the first lands. Each `summarizeNow` plans, sees the target still not current, and pays.

**Folders.** An opened folder whose files have no summaries yet gets a Sonnet call over "(no summary yet)" lines: money for a summary that says nothing.

**The fix:**
- Concurrent calls for the same target in one process share one promise. All the Map's calls go through the one background worker, so that covers them.
- A folder whose children aren't settled is refused with `TargetNotReadyError`.
- Each call ends by refreshing the summaries stage (Task 5).

**Files:**
- Modify: `lib/index/summarize/run.ts`, `scripts/summarize.ts`
- Test: `lib/index/summarize/run.test.ts`
- Docs: `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-033 amendment)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `TargetNotReadyError`.
  - `summarizeNow(repoRoot, targetPath, summarizer): Promise<StoredSummary>` keeps its signature. It now dedupes concurrent calls and refreshes the stage.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — in `lib/index/summarize/run.test.ts`:

```ts

/** Thrown by summarizeNow for a folder whose files or subfolders don't all have summaries yet. */
export class TargetNotReadyError extends Error {
    constructor(targetPath: string){
        super(`"${targetPath}" can't be summarized yet: some of its files or subfolders have no summary, and a folder's summary is written from theirs. Run npm run summarize, or open them first.`);
        this.name = "TargetNotReadyError";
    }
}
```
  3. Replace the whole `summarizeNow` function and its doc comment with:
```ts
/**
 * summarizeNow calls running in this process, by repo and path. A second call for a target already being summarized
 * joins the first instead of paying again: the Map can ask twice (a double click, a second tab) before the answer lands.
 */
const summarizingNow = new Map<string, Promise<StoredSummary>>();

/**
 * Summarizes one file or folder right now through the regular API, jumping the queue (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.3: the Map calls
 * this when someone opens a path that has no summary, or an out-of-date one). A current summary comes back as it is,
 * without an API call. A folder whose children aren't all summarized is refused (TargetNotReadyError): its summary would
 * be written over "(no summary yet)" lines. Concurrent calls for one target share one request (ADR-033, amended).
 */
export function summarizeNow(repoRoot: string, targetPath: string, summarizer: Summarizer): Promise<StoredSummary> {
    const root = path.resolve(repoRoot);
    const key = `${repoKey(root)}\0${targetPath}`;
    const running = summarizingNow.get(key);
    if (running) return running;

    const started = summarizeOnce(root, targetPath, summarizer).finally(() => summarizingNow.delete(key));
    summarizingNow.set(key, started);
    return started;
}

async function summarizeOnce(root: string, targetPath: string, summarizer: Summarizer): Promise<StoredSummary> {
    const db = getDb();
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(root));
    requireStructureReady(db, repoId);
    const snap = planSnapshot(db, repoId, true, new Set());
    const target = snap.targets.find((t) => t.path === targetPath);
    if (!target) throw new TargetNotFoundError(targetPath);
    if (target.state === "waiting") throw new TargetNotReadyError(targetPath);

    const ids = applyTargetUpdates(db, repoId, [target]);

    if (target.state !== "current" && target.state !== "template"){
        const ctx = newContext(db, repoId, root, () => {});
        const built = buildRequest(ctx, snap, target, ids);
        const outcome = "failure" in built ? built.failure : await summarizer.summarize(built.request);
        const destinations = destinationsFor([target], ids);
        ingest(ctx, [outcome], "direct", (customId) => destinations.get(customId));
    }

    // this summary moved the stage's counts, and its folder may now be out of date (ADR-032, amended in V1.3)
    refreshSummaryStatus(root, { summarizerAvailable: true });
    return loadSummaries(db, repoId).find((s) => s.kind === target.kind && s.path === target.path)!;
}
```
  4. In `scripts/summarize.ts`:
     - add `TargetNotReadyError` to the `../lib/index/summarize/run` import;
     - add it to the first `if` of `main().catch(...)`, next to `TargetNotFoundError`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Run them to make sure they pass** — `npx vitest run lib/index/summarize/run.test.ts`. Expected: PASS, the existing tests included.

- [ ] **Step 5: Document it.** At the end of ADR-033's **Decision** list, add a bullet:
  - **"Summarize now" can't pay twice.** *(Added ‹today› in slice V1.3, from V1.2's review.)* Concurrent `summarizeNow` calls for one target in one process share one promise and one request. The Map's requests all go through the one background worker (ADR-038), so a double click or two tabs pay once. A CLI `--path` running at the same moment is a separate process and isn't covered; it's rare, and costs one request. A folder whose children aren't all summarized is refused (`TargetNotReadyError`): it would otherwise be written over "(no summary yet)" lines for the price of a Sonnet call. Every call ends by refreshing the summaries stage (ADR-032).

### Task 8: A batch that keeps erroring has a way out (V1.2 deferred item 4)

**The bug.** Say one batch's results can never be downloaded: a malformed result line the SDK can't parse, or a 5xx on that one batch. Each poll counts as a missed poll, and the run gives up after 5 passes in a row. The rows stay claimed by the batch, so no later run resends them, and every run gives up at the same batch. Only a 404 (`BatchGoneError`) ever released a batch.

**The fix:** an explicit, paid-for-again way out.
- The stage note names the unreadable batch and the exact command.
- `npm run summarize -- <repo> --release-batch <id>` marks the batch's rows failed, so the run sends them again.

An automatic release was rejected (ADR-033's amendment): Anthropic keeps results for 29 days, and a network outage looks the same as a broken batch, so a timer would pay twice for answers that were only late.

**Files:**
- Modify: `lib/index/summarize/run.ts`, `lib/db/store/summaries.ts`, `scripts/summarize.ts`
- Test: `lib/index/summarize/run.test.ts`
- Docs: `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-033 amendment), `docs/RAG_PIPELINE.md` (§8)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `SummaryRunOptions.releaseBatches?: readonly string[]`.
  - `UnknownBatchError`.
  - `BatchesUnreachableError` (`batchIds`; its message is the last poll error's).
  - `releaseHint(root, batchIds): string`.
  - `countBatchRows(db, repoId, batchId): number`.
  - `releaseBatch(db, repoId, batchId, reason): number`.

```ts

/** How many of this repo's requests a batch carries. */
export function countBatchRows(db: Db, repoId: number, batchId: string): number {
    return db.select({ n: sql<number>`count(*)` }).from(summaries)
        .where(and(eq(summaries.repoId, repoId), eq(summaries.batchId, batchId))).get()?.n ?? 0;
}

/**
 * Gives up on a batch whose results can't be read (ADR-033, amended in V1.3). Its rows in this repo are marked failed
 * with `reason` and unclaimed, so the next pass sends them again, and they're paid for again. If the batch's own
 * answers ever arrive, they no longer land: the batch doesn't own the rows any more. Returns how many it released.
 */
export function releaseBatch(db: Db, repoId: number, batchId: string, reason: string): number {
    const rows = db.select({ id: summaries.id, pendingHash: summaries.pendingHash }).from(summaries)
        .where(and(eq(summaries.repoId, repoId), eq(summaries.batchId, batchId))).all();
    return writeResults(db, rows.map((r) => ({ id: r.id, batchId, inputHash: r.pendingHash ?? "", outcome: { ok: false as const, error: reason } })));
}
```

```ts
     * Batches to give up on before the run starts (ADR-033, amended in V1.3). Their requests are marked failed and sent
     * again, and paid for again: for a batch whose results keep failing to download. The stage note names such a batch.

```
  3. After `StructureNotReadyError`, add:
```ts

/** Thrown when --release-batch names a batch that carries none of this repo's requests. */
export class UnknownBatchError extends Error {
    constructor(readonly batchId: string){
        super(`No request of this repo is waiting in batch ${batchId}. Copy the id from the summaries stage's note.`);
        this.name = "UnknownBatchError";
    }
}

/**
 * Thrown when no batch could be checked MAX_MISSED_POLLS passes in a row. The message is the last poll's error; the ids
 * say which batches stayed unreadable, so the stage note can say how to give up on them (ADR-033, amended in V1.3).
 */
export class BatchesUnreachableError extends Error {
    constructor(readonly batchIds: readonly string[], cause: unknown){
        super(messageOf(cause), { cause });
        this.name = "BatchesUnreachableError";
    }
}

/** How to give up on batches whose results keep failing to download: their requests go out again, and are paid for again. */
export function releaseHint(root: string, batchIds: readonly string[]): string {
    const one = batchIds.length === 1;
    const flags = batchIds.map((id) => `--release-batch ${id}`).join(" ");
    return `if ${one ? "that batch" : "those batches"} can never be read, run npm run summarize -- "${repoKey(root)}" ${flags} to send ${one ? "its" : "their"} requests again (paid again)`;
}
```

```ts
    }
```

```ts

```
  8. Replace `reportRunError` with:
```ts
/**
 * The stage after a run stopped on an error. Requests still at Anthropic go on without this process, and the next run
 * collects them, so the stage stays "running" (ADR-032's exception) instead of calling paid-for work failed. When the
 * error was batches that couldn't be read, the note says how to give up on them.
 */
function reportRunError(db: Db, repoId: number, root: string, targets: readonly PlannedTarget[], err: unknown): void {
    try{
        const inFlight = countInFlight(db, repoId);
        const notes = [messageOf(err)];
        if (inFlight > 0) notes.push(stillAtAnthropic(inFlight));
        if (err instanceof BatchesUnreachableError) notes.push(releaseHint(root, err.batchIds));
        writeStage(db, repoId, "summaries", {
            state: inFlight > 0 ? "running" : "failed",
            done: upToDate(targets),
            total: targets.length,
            note: notes.join("; "),
        });
    } catch{
        // the database itself may be what failed (SQLITE_BUSY, say); the original error matters more
    }
}
```

### Task 9: The read routes: level, describe, search, status

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { IndexLockedError } from "../db/store/lock";
import { RepoNotIndexedError } from "../db/store/structure";
import { StructureNotReadyError, TargetNotFoundError, TargetNotReadyError, UnknownBatchError } from "../index/summarize/run";
import { errorResponseFor, NoActiveRepoError, SummariesOffError } from "./errors";
import { NotAFolderError, PathNotFoundError } from "./level";

describe("errorResponseFor", () => {
    it("gives each engine error its code and status, and keeps its message", () => {
        const cases: [Error, string, number][] = [
            [new RepoNotIndexedError("/r"), "INDEX_EMPTY", 404],
            [new NoActiveRepoError(), "INDEX_EMPTY", 404],
            [new PathNotFoundError("x"), "NOT_FOUND", 404],
            [new TargetNotFoundError("x"), "NOT_FOUND", 404],
            [new UnknownBatchError("b"), "NOT_FOUND", 404],
            [new NotAFolderError("a/b.ts"), "BAD_REQUEST", 400],
            [new IndexLockedError("2026-10-12T00:00:00.000Z"), "INDEX_LOCKED", 409],
            [new StructureNotReadyError("running"), "INDEX_LOCKED", 409],
            [new TargetNotReadyError("src"), "NOT_READY", 409],
            [new SummariesOffError(), "SUMMARIES_OFF", 409],
        ];
        for (const [err, code, status] of cases){
            expect(errorResponseFor(err)).toEqual({ status, error: { code, message: err.message } });
        }
    });

    it("calls anything else an internal error and says what it was", () => {
        expect(errorResponseFor(new Error("disk full"))).toEqual({ status: 500, error: { code: "INTERNAL", message: "Internal error: disk full" } });
        expect(errorResponseFor("odd")).toEqual({ status: 500, error: { code: "INTERNAL", message: "Internal error: odd" } });
    });
});
```

```ts
  | "BAD_REQUEST"
  | "FORBIDDEN"
  | "NOT_FOUND"
  | "INDEX_EMPTY"
```

- [ ] **Step 4: `lib/engine/errors.ts`:**
```ts
import type { ErrorCode } from "../contracts";
import { IndexLockedError } from "../db/store/lock";
import { RepoNotIndexedError } from "../db/store/structure";
import { StructureNotReadyError, TargetNotFoundError, TargetNotReadyError, UnknownBatchError } from "../index/summarize/run";
import { NotAFolderError, PathNotFoundError } from "./level";

/** Thrown when a summary is asked for and there's no API key to write it with. */
export class SummariesOffError extends Error {
    constructor(){
        super("Summaries are off: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set (put it in .env.local and restart the server).");
        this.name = "SummariesOffError";
    }
}

/** Thrown when there's no active repo: nothing has been indexed yet. */
export class NoActiveRepoError extends Error {
    constructor(){
        super("No repository indexed yet. Run npm run index -- <path-to-repo>.");
        this.name = "NoActiveRepoError";
    }
}

/** Each error code's HTTP status (API_CONTRACTS.md §1). */
export const STATUS_FOR: Readonly<Record<ErrorCode, number>> = {
    BAD_REQUEST: 400,
    FORBIDDEN: 403,
    NOT_FOUND: 404,
    INDEX_EMPTY: 404,
    INDEX_LOCKED: 409,
    NOT_READY: 409,
    SUMMARIES_OFF: 409,
    BUDGET_EXCEEDED: 429,
    UPSTREAM: 502,
    INTERNAL: 500,
};

/**
 * What an engine error means over HTTP: its code, its status, and the message to show. Errors are told apart by their
 * type, never their wording, so rewording a message can't change a code. Anything unexpected is INTERNAL.
 */
export function errorResponseFor(err: unknown): { status: number; error: { code: ErrorCode; message: string } } {
    const code = codeFor(err);
    const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
    return { status: STATUS_FOR[code], error: { code, message: code === "INTERNAL" ? `Internal error: ${message}` : message } };
}

function codeFor(err: unknown): ErrorCode {
    if (err instanceof RepoNotIndexedError || err instanceof NoActiveRepoError) return "INDEX_EMPTY";
    if (err instanceof PathNotFoundError || err instanceof TargetNotFoundError || err instanceof UnknownBatchError) return "NOT_FOUND";
    if (err instanceof NotAFolderError) return "BAD_REQUEST";
    if (err instanceof IndexLockedError || err instanceof StructureNotReadyError) return "INDEX_LOCKED";
    if (err instanceof TargetNotReadyError) return "NOT_READY";
    if (err instanceof SummariesOffError) return "SUMMARIES_OFF";
    return "INTERNAL";
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: The route helpers** — `app/api/_lib/respond.ts`. A folder starting with `_` is private in the App Router: never a route.
```ts
import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
import type { ErrorCode, ErrorEnvelope } from "@/lib/contracts";
import { readConfig } from "@/lib/config";
import { errorResponseFor } from "@/lib/engine/errors";

/** Shared by the route handlers: the error envelope (API_CONTRACTS.md §1) and the active repo. */
export function jsonError(status: number, code: ErrorCode, message: string): NextResponse<ErrorEnvelope> {
    return NextResponse.json<ErrorEnvelope>({ error: { code, message } }, { status });
}

/** An engine error as its envelope. Unexpected ones are logged here, where the stack is still at hand. */
export function errorJson(err: unknown): NextResponse<ErrorEnvelope> {
    const { status, error } = errorResponseFor(err);
    if (status === 500) console.error(err);
    return NextResponse.json<ErrorEnvelope>({ error }, { status });
}

/** The repo the Map shows: `activeRepo` in .nebulosity/config.json, which npm run index sets. */
export function activeRepo(): string | null {
    return readConfig().activeRepo;
}

export function noActiveRepo(): NextResponse<ErrorEnvelope> {
    return jsonError(404, "INDEX_EMPTY", "No repository indexed yet. Run npm run index -- <path-to-repo>.");
}

export function badPath(): NextResponse<ErrorEnvelope> {
    return jsonError(400, "BAD_REQUEST", "path must be repo-relative: forward slashes, no '..', and empty for the repo root.");
}

export function forbidden(): NextResponse<ErrorEnvelope> {
    return jsonError(403, "FORBIDDEN", "This endpoint only takes requests from this machine, and from no other web page.");
}
```

```ts
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from "next/server";
import type { LevelResponse } from "@/lib/contracts";
import { level } from "@/lib/engine/level";
import { parseRepoPath } from "@/lib/index/paths";
import { summariesEnabled } from "@/lib/index/summarize/env";
import { activeRepo, badPath, errorJson, noActiveRepo } from "../_lib/respond";

export const runtime = "nodejs";

// GET /api/level?path= — one folder of the Map, folded to 50 boxes (API_CONTRACTS.md §11)
export function GET(request: NextRequest){
    const repo = activeRepo();
    if (repo === null) return noActiveRepo();
    const folder = parseRepoPath(request.nextUrl.searchParams.get("path") ?? "");
    if (folder === null) return badPath();

    try{
        return NextResponse.json<LevelResponse>(level(repo, folder, { summariesOff: !summariesEnabled() }));
    } catch (err){
        return errorJson(err);
    }
}
```

```ts
export const runtime = "nodejs";

    const repo = activeRepo();
    if (repo === null) return noActiveRepo();

    } catch (err){
        return errorJson(err);
    }
}
```

  3. Replace `app/api/search/route.ts` with:
```ts
import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from "next/server";
import type { SearchResult } from "@/lib/contracts";
import { search } from "@/lib/engine/search";
import { activeRepo, errorJson, jsonError, noActiveRepo } from "../_lib/respond";

export const runtime = "nodejs";

// GET /api/search?q=&limit= — hybrid search over the active repo: files and folders, fused with RRF (API_CONTRACTS.md §10)
export async function GET(request: NextRequest){
    const repo = activeRepo();
    if (repo === null) return noActiveRepo();
    const query = request.nextUrl.searchParams.get("q")?.trim() ?? "";
    if (query === "") return jsonError(400, "BAD_REQUEST", "Missing query parameter 'q'.");
    // 10 by default, at most 50: each list contributes 50 at most anyway
    const limit = Math.min(50, Math.max(1, Number(request.nextUrl.searchParams.get("limit")) || 10));

    try{
        return NextResponse.json<SearchResult>(await search(repo, query, { limit }));
    } catch (err){
        return errorJson(err);
    }
}
```
  `GET /api/status` keeps its route; it now carries `indexVersion` (Task 5).

### Task 10: Incremental structure updates (S6)

S6 asks for ≤ 2 s from saving a file until `level`/`describe` show it.

**Why a full run can't do it.** A warm full run takes 4.9 s on VS Code: 2.1 s to discover and hash, 2.7 s to re-resolve and rewrite every import and aggregate. Measured while writing this plan, the in-memory part of those 2.7 s is small: resolving all 151,527 specifiers takes about 0.33 s and rebuilding the level edges 0.25 s. The rest is the database rewriting 139,580 import rows and 19,345 level-edge rows that are almost all unchanged. And the 1 s debounce comes out of the same 2 s.

**The update:**
- **An edited file.** Re-parse it, re-resolve its own imports, and write only the differences. A level edge's count moves by ±1 per import, because where an import is counted depends only on its two paths.
- **A created or deleted file, or a changed tsconfig.** Re-resolve every file in memory, since a new file can be what `../core` now means. Still write only the differences.

The tests check every scenario against a full run of the same tree.

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/index/update.ts`, `lib/index/update.test.ts`
- Modify:
  - `lib/index/discover.ts` and `lib/index/discover.test.ts`
  - `lib/index/aggregate.ts` and `lib/index/aggregate.test.ts`
  - `lib/index/resolve.ts`
  - `lib/index/pipeline.ts`
  - `lib/db/store/structure.ts`
- Docs:
  - `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md` (ADR-037)
  - `docs/DATABASE_DESIGN.md` (§9)
  - `docs/RAG_PIPELINE.md` (§7)
  - `docs/PIVOT_SPEC.md` (§5.6)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - In `lib/index/discover.ts`: `IGNORED_DIRS` (now exported), `isExcludedPath(rel, { extensions, usedGit }): boolean`, `gitIgnored(repoRoot, rels): Set<string>`.
  - `levelEdgeDeltas(removed, added): LevelEdgeRow[]` (counts may be negative).
  - `edgesFrom(resolve: Resolver, source: string, specifiers: readonly string[]): ImportEdge[]`.
  - `toFileRecord(filePath, text, contentHash, sizeBytes): FileRecord` (pipeline.ts).
  - In `lib/db/store/structure.ts`:
    - `loadFileIds(db, repoId, paths): Map<string, number>`;
    - `loadImportEdges(db, repoId): ImportEdge[]`;
    - `loadImportEdgesFrom(db, repoId, paths): ImportEdge[]`;
    - `loadImportEdgesTouching(db, repoId, paths): ImportEdge[]`;
    - `deleteFiles(db, repoId, paths): string[]`;
    - `applyEdgeChanges(db, repoId, removed, added, deltas): void`;
    - `syncFolders(db, repoId, rows): void`.
  - `type FileChangeResult`, and `applyFileChanges(repoRoot: string, changedPaths: readonly string[], options?: { maxFileBytes?: number }): FileChangeResult`.

```ts
describe("isExcludedPath", () => {
    it("leaves out what discoverFiles would, whatever the file holds", () => {
        const git = { extensions: TS_EXTENSIONS, usedGit: true };
        const walk = { extensions: TS_EXTENSIONS, usedGit: false };
        expect(isExcludedPath("src/a.ts", git)).toBe(false);
        expect(isExcludedPath("README.md", git)).toBe(true);
        expect(isExcludedPath(".env.ts", git)).toBe(true);
        expect(isExcludedPath("node_modules/x/a.ts", git)).toBe(true);
        expect(isExcludedPath("a/.git/b.ts", git)).toBe(true);
        expect(isExcludedPath("build/gulpfile.ts", git)).toBe(false);   // in a git repo, .gitignore decides
        expect(isExcludedPath("build/gulpfile.ts", walk)).toBe(true);
        expect(isExcludedPath("packages/p/dist/e.js", walk)).toBe(true);
    });
});

describe("gitIgnored", () => {
    it("asks git once which paths .gitignore excludes", () => {
        const dir = makeTempDir("neb-ignored-");
        execFileSync("git", ["init", "-q", dir]);
        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, ".gitignore"), "out/\n*.gen.ts\n");
        expect([...gitIgnored(dir, ["out/a.ts", "src/b.gen.ts", "src/c.ts"])].sort()).toEqual(["out/a.ts", "src/b.gen.ts"]);
        expect(gitIgnored(dir, ["src/c.ts"]).size).toBe(0);
        expect(gitIgnored(dir, []).size).toBe(0);
    });
});
```

  3. **`lib/index/update.test.ts`:**
```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { execFileSync } from "child_process";
import { and, eq } from "drizzle-orm";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { copyFixture } from "../../test/helpers/fixture";
import { stubSummarizer } from "../../test/helpers/summarizer";
import { makeTempDir } from "../../test/helpers/temp";
import { getDb } from "../db/client";
import { folders, levelEdges } from "../db/schema";
import { acquireIndexLock, IndexLockedError, releaseIndexLock } from "../db/store/lock";
import { loadImportEdges } from "../db/store/structure";
import { loadFileFacts, loadFolderFacts, loadSummaries } from "../db/store/summaries";
import { readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { indexRepository } from "./pipeline";
import { runSummaries } from "./summarize/run";
import { applyFileChanges } from "./update";

const write = (root: string, rel: string, text: string): void => {
    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(path.join(root, rel)), { recursive: true });
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(root, rel), text);
};
const prepend = (root: string, rel: string, text: string): void =>
    write(root, rel, text + fs.readFileSync(path.join(root, rel), "utf8"));

/** Everything a structure run derives, in a form two repos can be compared by (the root folder's name differs). */
function tables(repoId: number){
    const db = getDb();
    return {
        files: loadFileFacts(db, repoId).map((f) => [f.path, f.contentHash, f.lineCount, f.isTest, f.importedBy]),
        imports: loadImportEdges(db, repoId).map((e) => `${e.source} -> ${e.target} (${e.specifier})`).sort(),
        levelEdges: db.select().from(levelEdges).where(eq(levelEdges.repoId, repoId)).all()
            .map((r) => `${r.levelPath} | ${r.sourceNode} -> ${r.targetNode} × ${r.count}`).sort(),
        folders: loadFolderFacts(db, repoId).map((f) => [f.path, f.parentPath, f.depth, f.fileCount, f.directFileCount]),
    };
}

/** The oracle: what a full run makes of the same tree, in a twin copy indexed as a repo of its own. */
function fullRunOf(root: string){
    const twin = makeTempDir("neb-twin-");
    fs.cpSync(root, twin, { recursive: true });
    return tables(indexRepository(twin).repoId);
}

function levelEdgeCount(repoId: number, levelPath: string, sourceNode: string, targetNode: string): number {
    return getDb().select({ count: levelEdges.count }).from(levelEdges).where(and(
        eq(levelEdges.repoId, repoId), eq(levelEdges.levelPath, levelPath), eq(levelEdges.sourceNode, sourceNode), eq(levelEdges.targetNode, targetNode),
    )).get()?.count ?? 0;
}

function folderCounts(repoId: number, paths: string[]): number[][] {
    return paths.map((p) => {
        const row = getDb().select().from(folders).where(and(eq(folders.repoId, repoId), eq(folders.path, p))).get()!;
        return [row.fileCount, row.directFileCount];
    });
}

describe("applyFileChanges", () => {
    it("an edit that adds an import re-resolves only that file and moves one level-edge count", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        prepend(root, "src/util/strings.ts", `import { banner } from "../core/log";\n`);

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/util/strings.ts"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: ["src/util/strings.ts"], deleted: [], unchanged: 0, resolvedAll: false, edgesAdded: 1, edgesRemoved: 0 });
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src", "src/util", "src/core")).toBe(2);
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("a new file re-resolves everything, and its folder and every folder above count it", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        write(root, "src/core/extra.ts", `import { log } from "./log";\nexport const extra = 1;\n`);

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/core/extra.ts"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: ["src/core/extra.ts"], deleted: [], resolvedAll: true, edgesAdded: 1, edgesRemoved: 0 });
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src/core", "src/core/extra.ts", "src/core/log.ts")).toBe(1);
        expect(folderCounts(repoId, ["", "src", "src/core"])).toEqual([[9, 0], [8, 1], [3, 3]]);
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("a deleted file takes its imports, its folders' counts and its summary with it", async () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        await runSummaries(root, { summarizer: stubSummarizer().summarizer, transport: "direct" });
        fs.rmSync(path.join(root, "src/core/index.ts"));

        // edges 7 (core/index → log) and 11 (strings → core/index) go with it; strings.ts's "../core" now resolves to nothing
        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/core/index.ts"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: [], deleted: ["src/core/index.ts"], resolvedAll: true, edgesAdded: 0, edgesRemoved: 2 });
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src/core", "src/core/index.ts", "src/core/log.ts")).toBe(0);
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src", "src/util", "src/core")).toBe(0);
        expect(folderCounts(repoId, ["", "src", "src/core"])).toEqual([[7, 0], [6, 1], [1, 1]]);
        expect(loadSummaries(getDb(), repoId).some((s) => s.path === "src/core/index.ts")).toBe(false);
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("a rename is a delete plus a create: the old name's imports stop resolving, the new file's own resolve", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        fs.renameSync(path.join(root, "src/app/view.tsx"), path.join(root, "src/app/panel.tsx"));

        // edges 4 and 6 go with view.tsx; panel.tsx imports ./main again; main.ts's ./view now resolves to nothing
        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/app/view.tsx", "src/app/panel.tsx"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: ["src/app/panel.tsx"], deleted: ["src/app/view.tsx"], resolvedAll: true, edgesAdded: 1, edgesRemoved: 2 });
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src/app", "src/app/panel.tsx", "src/app/main.ts")).toBe(1);
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src/app", "src/app/main.ts", "src/app/view.tsx")).toBe(0);
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("a changed tsconfig re-resolves every file: without the @core alias, src/index.ts's import of it goes", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(root, "tsconfig.json"), `{ "compilerOptions": { "baseUrl": "." } }\n`);

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["tsconfig.json"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: [], deleted: [], unchanged: 1, resolvedAll: true, edgesAdded: 0, edgesRemoved: 1 });
        expect(levelEdgeCount(repoId, "src", "src/index.ts", "src/core")).toBe(0);
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("does nothing for a file whose bytes didn't change, nor for paths that aren't source files", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        const version = readIndexVersion(getDb(), repoId);

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/core/log.ts", "README.md", "docs/notes.md"]))
            .toMatchObject({ changed: [], deleted: [], unchanged: 3, edgesAdded: 0, edgesRemoved: 0, indexVersion: version });
    });

    it("keeps a new file .gitignore excludes out of the index, as a full run would", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        execFileSync("git", ["init", "-q", root]);
        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(root, ".gitignore"), "generated/\n");
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        write(root, "generated/out.ts", "export const x = 1;\n");
        write(root, "src/fresh.ts", "export const y = 1;\n");

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["generated/out.ts", "src/fresh.ts"])).toMatchObject({ changed: ["src/fresh.ts"], unchanged: 1 });
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("a folder deleted as one path (a watcher's unlinkDir) removes every file in it", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        const { repoId } = indexRepository(root);
        fs.rmSync(path.join(root, "src/util"), { recursive: true });

        expect(applyFileChanges(root, ["src/util"])).toMatchObject({ deleted: ["src/util/strings.test.ts", "src/util/strings.ts"] });
        expect(tables(repoId)).toEqual(fullRunOf(root));
    });

    it("refuses to run while another structure run holds the index lock", () => {
        const root = copyFixture();
        indexRepository(root);
        const db = getDb();
        acquireIndexLock(db);
        try{
            expect(() => applyFileChanges(root, ["src/core/log.ts"])).toThrow(IndexLockedError);
        } finally{
            releaseIndexLock(db);
        }
    });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Discovery helpers** — in `lib/index/discover.ts`:
  - make the ignored folders exported and read-only: `export const IGNORED_DIRS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(["node_modules", ".git", ".next", ".nebulosity"]);`;
  - append:
```ts

/**
 * Whether discoverFiles would leave this path out whatever its contents: a .env file, an extension no adapter claims,
 * or a folder it never enters (plus build output when walking). The incremental update (update.ts) asks this of every
 * path a watcher reports, so a file is in the index exactly when a full run would put it there.
 */
export function isExcludedPath(rel: string, opts: { extensions: readonly string[]; usedGit: boolean }): boolean {
    const segments = rel.split("/");
    const base = segments[segments.length - 1];
    if (base.startsWith(".env") || !opts.extensions.some((ext) => rel.endsWith(ext))) return true;

    const parents = segments.slice(0, -1);
    if (parents.some((segment) => IGNORED_DIRS.has(segment))) return true;
    return !opts.usedGit && parents.some((segment) => WALK_ONLY_IGNORED_DIRS.has(segment));
}

/**
 * Which of these paths .gitignore excludes, asked of git once for all of them. Tracked files are never reported, by
 * git's own rule, which matches ls-files. Exit status 1 means none is ignored; a git that can't answer ignores nothing.
 */
export function gitIgnored(repoRoot: string, rels: readonly string[]): Set<string> {
    if (rels.length === 0) return new Set();

    try{
        const out = execFileSync("git", ["-C", repoRoot, "check-ignore", "-z", "--stdin"], {
            input: rels.join("\0"),
            stdio: ["pipe", "pipe", "ignore"],
            maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
        });
        return new Set(out.toString("utf8").split("\0").filter(Boolean).map(normalizeSlashes));
    } catch{
        return new Set();
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Level-edge deltas** — in `lib/index/aggregate.ts`, replace `buildLevelEdges` with the two functions below, which share where an import is counted:
```ts
/** Where one import is counted on the Map (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2): the level, and the two boxes it joins there. */
function placeImport(source: string, target: string): Omit<LevelEdgeRow, "count"> {
    const levelPath = lcaFolder(dirOf(source), dirOf(target));
    return { levelPath, sourceNode: childToward(levelPath, source), targetNode: childToward(levelPath, target) };
}

const byRow = (a: LevelEdgeRow, b: LevelEdgeRow): number =>
    compare(a.levelPath, b.levelPath) || compare(a.sourceNode, b.sourceNode) || compare(a.targetNode, b.targetNode);

/**
 * The Map's lines (PIVOT_SPEC.md §4.2). Each import is counted exactly once, at the
 * level where it crosses between siblings, so opening any folder needs one indexed
 * lookup and no computation. VS Code's 20,648 workbench → base imports become one row.
 */
export function buildLevelEdges(edges: readonly { source: string; target: string }[]): LevelEdgeRow[] {
    const rows = new Map<string, LevelEdgeRow>();

    for (const { source, target } of edges){
        const place = placeImport(source, target);
        const key = `${place.levelPath}\0${place.sourceNode}\0${place.targetNode}`;
        const row = rows.get(key);
        if (row) row.count++;
        else rows.set(key, { ...place, count: 1 });
    }

    return [...rows.values()].sort(byRow);
}

/**
 * How the level edges change when imports come and go (ADR-037): −1 for each removed import and +1 for each added one,
 * on the row each is counted on, netted out. Where an import is counted depends only on its two paths, so these deltas
 * applied to the stored rows give exactly what buildLevelEdges would build from the new set of imports.
 */
export function levelEdgeDeltas(removed: readonly { source: string; target: string }[], added: readonly { source: string; target: string }[]): LevelEdgeRow[] {
    const rows = new Map<string, LevelEdgeRow>();
    const shift = (source: string, target: string, by: number): void => {
        const place = placeImport(source, target);
        const key = `${place.levelPath}\0${place.sourceNode}\0${place.targetNode}`;
        const row = rows.get(key);
        if (row) row.count += by;
        else rows.set(key, { ...place, count: by });
    };

    for (const e of removed) shift(e.source, e.target, -1);
    for (const e of added) shift(e.source, e.target, 1);
    return [...rows.values()].filter((r) => r.count !== 0).sort(byRow);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: One edge rule, one record builder.**
  1. **`lib/index/resolve.ts`.** Add `import type { ImportEdge } from "../db/store/structure";` and append:
```ts

/**
 * One file's import edges: each specifier resolved, one edge per target file, the first specifier that reaches it
 * naming it. The full run and the incremental update both build edges here, so they can't disagree.
 */
export function edgesFrom(resolve: Resolver, source: string, specifiers: readonly string[]): ImportEdge[] {
    const seen = new Set<string>();
    const edges: ImportEdge[] = [];

    for (const specifier of specifiers){
        const target = resolve(source, specifier);
        if (target === null || seen.has(target)) continue;   // one edge per file pair
        seen.add(target);
        edges.push({ source, target, specifier });
    }

    return edges;
}
```
  2. **`lib/index/pipeline.ts`.**
     - Change `import { createResolver } from "./resolve";` to `import { createResolver, edgesFrom } from "./resolve";`.
     - Replace the resolve loop (from `for (const [source, file] of current){` to its closing brace) with:
```ts

```
     - Append:
```ts

/** One file's row as the structure store writes it, from its bytes (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.2). Throws if the file can't be parsed. */
export function toFileRecord(filePath: string, text: string, contentHash: string, sizeBytes: number): FileRecord {
    const parsed = parseTypeScriptFile(filePath, text);
    return {
        path: filePath,
        kind: /\.(tsx|jsx)$/.test(filePath) ? "component" : "module",
        contentHash,
        language: languageOf(filePath),
        sizeBytes,
        lineCount: parsed.lineCount,
        isTest: isTestPath(filePath),
        importSpecifiers: parsed.imports,
        chunks: parsed.chunks.map((c) => ({ ...c, nameSplit: splitIdentifier(c.name) })),
        symbols: parsed.symbols,
    };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: The store's incremental writes** — in `lib/db/store/structure.ts`:
  1. Change the imports to:
```ts
import { and, eq, inArray, lte, or, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
import { alias } from "drizzle-orm/sqlite-core";
import type { Db } from "../client";
import {
    chunks, files, folders, imports, levelEdges, repositories, symbols,
    type FolderRow, type LevelEdgeRow, type SymbolRow,
} from "../schema";
import { chunkRows, columnCount, forEachBatch } from "../batch";
import type { CodeChunk } from "../../contracts";
```
  2. Append:
```ts

/** Paths per IN (…) list: far under the parameter cap, with room for the other bound values. */
const IN_LIST = 900;

/** Paths → file ids in this repo, looked up in chunks under the parameter cap. Paths that aren't indexed are left out. */
export function loadFileIds(db: Db, repoId: number, paths: readonly string[]): Map<string, number> {
    const ids = new Map<string, number>();
    for (const batch of chunkRows([...new Set(paths)], 1, IN_LIST)){
        const rows = db.select({ id: files.id, path: files.path }).from(files)
            .where(and(eq(files.repoId, repoId), inArray(files.path, batch))).all();
        for (const row of rows) ids.set(row.path, row.id);
    }
    return ids;
}

/** Imports as paths, filtered by `where` over the source and target files (aliased; imports never cross repos). */
function selectImportEdges(db: Db, repoId: number, where?: (source: typeof files, target: typeof files) => ReturnType<typeof and>): ImportEdge[] {
    const source = alias(files, "source");
    const target = alias(files, "target");
    return db.select({ source: source.path, target: target.path, specifier: imports.specifier })
        .from(imports)
        .innerJoin(source, eq(source.id, imports.sourceFileId))
        .innerJoin(target, eq(target.id, imports.targetFileId))
        .where(and(eq(source.repoId, repoId), where?.(source as unknown as typeof files, target as unknown as typeof files)))
        .all();
}

/** Every import of a repo, as paths. The incremental update diffs against these when the file set changed. */
export function loadImportEdges(db: Db, repoId: number): ImportEdge[] {
    return selectImportEdges(db, repoId);
}

/** The imports out of these files. */
export function loadImportEdgesFrom(db: Db, repoId: number, paths: readonly string[]): ImportEdge[] {
    return chunkRows([...new Set(paths)], 1, IN_LIST).flatMap((batch) =>
        selectImportEdges(db, repoId, (source) => inArray(source.path, batch)));
}

/** The imports into or out of these files: the ones deleting the files would cascade away. */
export function loadImportEdgesTouching(db: Db, repoId: number, paths: readonly string[]): ImportEdge[] {
    const seen = new Map<string, ImportEdge>();
    for (const batch of chunkRows([...new Set(paths)], 1, IN_LIST)){
        for (const edge of selectImportEdges(db, repoId, (source, target) => or(inArray(source.path, batch), inArray(target.path, batch)))){
            seen.set(`${edge.source}\0${edge.target}`, edge);
        }
    }
    return [...seen.values()];
}

/** Deletes these files (their chunks, symbols and imports cascade) and returns the paths that were indexed, in order. */
export function deleteFiles(db: Db, repoId: number, paths: readonly string[]): string[] {
    const ids = loadFileIds(db, repoId, paths);
    db.transaction((tx) => {
        for (const id of ids.values()) tx.delete(files).where(eq(files.id, id)).run();
    });
    return [...ids.keys()].sort();
}

/**
 * An incremental update of the import graph and the Map's lines (ADR-037), in one transaction so no reader sees half
 * of it. The removed imports go and the added ones arrive (a removed and an added row for the same pair, when only
 * the specifier changed). Each level edge moves by its delta, and a row that reaches 0 is deleted.
 */
export function applyEdgeChanges(
    db: Db, repoId: number, removed: readonly ImportEdge[], added: readonly ImportEdge[], deltas: readonly LevelEdgeRow[]
): void {
    const ids = loadFileIds(db, repoId, [...removed, ...added].flatMap((e) => [e.source, e.target]));
    const idsOf = (e: ImportEdge): { sourceFileId: number; targetFileId: number } | null => {
        const sourceFileId = ids.get(e.source);
        const targetFileId = ids.get(e.target);
        return sourceFileId === undefined || targetFileId === undefined ? null : { sourceFileId, targetFileId };
    };

    db.transaction((tx) => {
        for (const edge of removed){
            const pair = idsOf(edge);
            if (pair) tx.delete(imports).where(and(eq(imports.sourceFileId, pair.sourceFileId), eq(imports.targetFileId, pair.targetFileId))).run();
        }

        const rows = added.flatMap((edge) => {
            const pair = idsOf(edge);
            return pair ? [{ ...pair, specifier: edge.specifier }] : [];
        });
        forEachBatch(rows, columnCount(imports), (batch) => { tx.insert(imports).values(batch).onConflictDoNothing().run(); });

        for (const delta of deltas){
            tx.insert(levelEdges).values({ repoId, ...delta })
                .onConflictDoUpdate({
                    target: [levelEdges.repoId, levelEdges.levelPath, levelEdges.sourceNode, levelEdges.targetNode],
                    set: { count: sql`${levelEdges.count} + ${delta.count}` },
                })
                .run();
        }
        tx.delete(levelEdges).where(and(eq(levelEdges.repoId, repoId), lte(levelEdges.count, 0))).run();
    });
}

/** Makes the folders table match `rows` (buildFolders over the current files), writing only what differs. */
export function syncFolders(db: Db, repoId: number, rows: readonly FolderRow[]): void {
    const stored = new Map(db.select().from(folders).where(eq(folders.repoId, repoId)).all().map((f) => [f.path, f]));
    const wanted = new Set(rows.map((r) => r.path));

    db.transaction((tx) => {
        for (const [folderPath, row] of stored) if (!wanted.has(folderPath)) tx.delete(folders).where(eq(folders.id, row.id)).run();

        const created = rows.filter((r) => !stored.has(r.path)).map((r) => ({ repoId, ...r }));
        forEachBatch(created, columnCount(folders), (batch) => { tx.insert(folders).values(batch).run(); });

        for (const r of rows){
            const row = stored.get(r.path);
            if (row && (row.fileCount !== r.fileCount || row.directFileCount !== r.directFileCount)){
                tx.update(folders).set({ fileCount: r.fileCount, directFileCount: r.directFileCount }).where(eq(folders.id, row.id)).run();
            }
        }
    });
}
```
     If the `where` callback's typing fights `alias` (Drizzle types aliased tables separately), inline the three queries instead of sharing `selectImportEdges`; the behavior must stay as written.

- [ ] **Step 7: The update** — `lib/index/update.ts`:
```ts
import { createHash } from "crypto";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";
import { getDb } from "../db/client";
import { acquireIndexLock, releaseIndexLock } from "../db/store/lock";
import { writeStage } from "../db/store/stages";
import {
    applyEdgeChanges, deleteFiles, loadImportEdges, loadImportEdgesFrom, loadImportEdgesTouching, loadStoredFiles,
    markIndexed, requireRepository, syncFolders, writeFileRecords,
    type FileError, type FileRecord, type ImportEdge,
} from "../db/store/structure";
import { pruneSummaries } from "../db/store/summaries";
import { bumpIndexVersion, readIndexVersion } from "../db/store/version";
import { buildFolders, levelEdgeDeltas } from "./aggregate";
import { DEFAULT_MAX_FILE_BYTES, gitIgnored, isExcludedPath, readGitHead } from "./discover";
import { TYPESCRIPT_EXTENSIONS } from "./languages/typescript";
import { normalizeSlashes, repoKey } from "./paths";
import { toFileRecord } from "./pipeline";
import { createResolver, edgesFrom } from "./resolve";

export type FileChangeResult = {
    repoId: number;
    /** Files whose new bytes are now indexed: edited or created. */
    changed: string[];
    /** Files that left the index: deleted, renamed away, now ignored, or grown past the size cap. */
    deleted: string[];
    /** Paths that needed nothing: the same bytes, or never a source file. */
    unchanged: number;
    errors: FileError[];
    /** Every file's imports were re-resolved: the file set changed, or a tsconfig did (ADR-037). */
    resolvedAll: boolean;
    edgesAdded: number;
    edgesRemoved: number;
    totalMs: number;
    /** The repo's index version afterwards: it moves only when something changed. */
    indexVersion: number;
};

/** A tsconfig decides what bare specifiers resolve to everywhere below it, so changing one re-resolves every file. */
const TSCONFIG = /(^|\/)tsconfig[^/]*\.json$/;

/**
 * The incremental structure update (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6, ADR-037): what the background worker runs for the paths a
 * watcher reported, as repo-relative paths. Each path is looked at as it is now.
 * - An edited file is re-parsed and its own imports re-resolved.
 * - A created or deleted file, or a changed tsconfig, re-resolves every file's imports in memory: a new file can be what
 *   "../core" now means.
 * Either way only the differences are written (import rows, level-edge counts, folder counts), so the result is what a
 * full run would store, without rewriting VS Code's 139,580 unchanged imports.
 */
export function applyFileChanges(repoRoot: string, changedPaths: readonly string[], options: { maxFileBytes?: number } = {}): FileChangeResult {
    const started = performance.now();
    const root = path.resolve(repoRoot);
    const db = getDb();
    const repoId = requireRepository(db, repoKey(root));
    const maxBytes = options.maxFileBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_FILE_BYTES;
    const usedGit = fs.existsSync(path.join(root, ".git"));
    const excluded = (rel: string): boolean => isExcludedPath(rel, { extensions: TYPESCRIPT_EXTENSIONS, usedGit });

    acquireIndexLock(db);
    try{
        const stored = loadStoredFiles(db, repoId);
        const paths = expandFolders(changedPaths, stored);
        // a new path .gitignore excludes never entered the index; one git call answers for all of them
        const ignored = usedGit ? gitIgnored(root, paths.filter((p) => !stored.has(p) && !excluded(p))) : new Set<string>();

        const records: FileRecord[] = [];
        const gone: string[] = [];
        const errors: FileError[] = [];
        let unchanged = 0;

        for (const rel of paths){
            const size = fileSize(path.join(root, rel));
            if (size === null || size > maxBytes || excluded(rel) || ignored.has(rel)){
                // not a source file now: if the index has it, it goes
                if (stored.has(rel)) gone.push(rel);
                else unchanged++;
                continue;
            }

            let text: string;
            try{
                text = fs.readFileSync(path.join(root, rel), "utf8");
            } catch (err){
                errors.push({ path: rel, error: messageOf(err) });
                continue;
            }

            const contentHash = createHash("sha256").update(text).digest("hex");
            // ADR-022: the hash means "the indexer last processed exactly these bytes"
            if (stored.get(rel)?.contentHash === contentHash){
                unchanged++;
                continue;
            }

            try{
                records.push(toFileRecord(rel, text, contentHash, size));
            } catch (err){
                errors.push({ path: rel, error: messageOf(err) });
            }
        }

        const writeErrors = writeFileRecords(db, repoId, records);
        errors.push(...writeErrors);
        const failed = new Set(writeErrors.map((e) => e.path));
        const changed = records.map((r) => r.path).filter((p) => !failed.has(p));

        // deleting a file cascades its imports away: capture them first, for the level-edge counts
        const cascaded = loadImportEdgesTouching(db, repoId, gone);
        const deleted = deleteFiles(db, repoId, gone);

        const fileSetChanged = changed.some((p) => !stored.has(p)) || deleted.length > 0;
        const resolvedAll = fileSetChanged || paths.some((p) => TSCONFIG.test(p));
        const current = loadStoredFiles(db, repoId);
        const resolve = createResolver(root, new Set(current.keys()));

        // the imports as they are now against as they were: every file's when the file set or a tsconfig changed,
        // else only the changed files' own (a file that failed keeps its old row and old edges, ADR-022)
        const sources = resolvedAll ? [...current.keys()] : changed;
        const before = resolvedAll ? loadImportEdges(db, repoId) : loadImportEdgesFrom(db, repoId, changed);
        const after = sources.flatMap((source) => edgesFrom(resolve, source, current.get(source)!.importSpecifiers));
        const { removed, added } = diffEdges(before, after);

        applyEdgeChanges(db, repoId, removed, added, levelEdgeDeltas([...removed, ...cascaded], added));
        if (fileSetChanged){
            syncFolders(db, repoId, buildFolders([...current.keys()], path.basename(root)));
            pruneSummaries(db, repoId);   // a deleted file, or a folder left empty, takes its summary with it
        }

        const anything = changed.length > 0 || deleted.length > 0 || removed.length > 0 || added.length > 0;
        if (anything){
            markIndexed(db, repoId, readGitHead(root));
            writeStage(db, repoId, "structure", {
                state: "ready",
                done: current.size,
                total: current.size,
                ...(errors.length > 0 ? { note: `${errors.length} files failed to index` } : {}),
            });
        }

        return {
            repoId,
            changed,
            deleted,
            unchanged,
            errors,
            resolvedAll,
            edgesAdded: added.length,
            edgesRemoved: removed.length + cascaded.length,
            totalMs: performance.now() - started,
            indexVersion: anything ? bumpIndexVersion(db, repoId) : readIndexVersion(db, repoId),
        };
    } finally{
        releaseIndexLock(db);
    }
}

/**
 * The paths to look at. A path the index doesn't hold may be a whole folder that was deleted or renamed (a watcher's
 * unlinkDir), so every indexed file under it is looked at too.
 */
function expandFolders(changedPaths: readonly string[], stored: ReadonlyMap<string, unknown>): string[] {
    const paths = new Set(changedPaths.map(normalizeSlashes));
    for (const rel of [...paths]){
        if (stored.has(rel)) continue;
        const prefix = `${rel}/`;
        for (const filePath of stored.keys()) if (filePath.startsWith(prefix)) paths.add(filePath);
    }
    return [...paths].sort();
}

/** The size of a regular file, or null when there's no file there (deleted, or a folder). */
function fileSize(absolute: string): number | null {
    try{
        const stat = fs.statSync(absolute);
        return stat.isFile() ? stat.size : null;
    } catch{
        return null;
    }
}

/** Import rows to delete and to insert. The specifier is part of the key, so a rewritten specifier replaces its row. */
function diffEdges(before: readonly ImportEdge[], after: readonly ImportEdge[]): { removed: ImportEdge[]; added: ImportEdge[] } {
    const key = (e: ImportEdge): string => `${e.source}\0${e.target}\0${e.specifier}`;
    const beforeKeys = new Set(before.map(key));
    const afterKeys = new Set(after.map(key));
    return {
        removed: before.filter((e) => !afterKeys.has(key(e))),
        added: after.filter((e) => !beforeKeys.has(key(e))),
    };
}

function messageOf(err: unknown): string {
    return err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Document it.**
  1. **ADR-037**, appended to `docs/ENGINEERING_DECISIONS.md`:
```md

## ADR-037 — Incremental structure updates write only what changed (‹today›)

**Context:** S6 asks for ≤ 2 s from saving a file until `level`/`describe` reflect it, and the watcher's 1 s debounce (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6) uses half of that. A warm full run takes 4.9 s on VS Code. Measured while planning V1.3, re-resolving all 151,527 specifiers in memory takes about 0.33 s and rebuilding the level edges 0.25 s. The rest of the run's 2.7 s of resolve and aggregate is the database rewriting 139,580 import rows and 19,345 level-edge rows, nearly all unchanged.
**Decision:** `applyFileChanges(root, paths)` looks at each reported path as it is now.
- **Edits.** An edited file is re-parsed, and only its own imports are re-resolved and diffed against its stored ones.
- **Creates, deletes, tsconfigs.** When a file is created or deleted, or a tsconfig changes, every file's imports are re-resolved in memory, because a new file can be what `../core` now means. The result is diffed against all stored imports.
- **Writes.** Only the differences are written. Import rows are deleted and inserted. Each level-edge count moves by its delta: an import is counted on a row decided by its two paths alone, so −1 per removed import and +1 per added one, netted, equals a rebuild. Folder rows are synced; summaries of vanished targets are pruned.
- **One rule.** Edges come from one shared rule (`edgesFrom`) and records from one builder (`toFileRecord`), both used by the full run too.
- **Exclusions.** A path is excluded exactly as discovery would exclude it (`isExcludedPath`). New paths are checked against `.gitignore` with one `git check-ignore --stdin` call.
- **Folders as one path.** A path the index doesn't hold may be a deleted folder (a watcher's `unlinkDir`); every indexed file under it is looked at.
**Alternatives:** (a) Run the full pipeline on every save: 4.9 s, so S6 fails. (b) Find the files that could import a new file by their specifiers, without re-resolving everything: faster, but every resolver rule (aliases, `baseUrl`, `index` files, NodeNext) would need an inverse, and an inverse that's wrong silently drops edges.
**Consequences:** A single-file edit costs tens of milliseconds on VS Code (measured in Task 22); a create or delete about half a second. The tests check every kind of change against a full run of the same tree, so the two paths can't drift apart.
**Revisit when:** a repo is big enough that the in-memory re-resolve of a create or delete passes 1 s (then index files by the specifier stems they could satisfy), or E2's call edges need the same treatment.
```

The background worker's structure updates write only differences: import rows deleted and inserted, `level_edges` counts moved by ±1 per import (a row that reaches 0 is deleted), folder rows synced, and summaries of vanished targets pruned. All of it in one transaction, apart from the file rows themselves.
  3. **`docs/RAG_PIPELINE.md` §7.** Replace the paragraph on incremental structure updates (or add one if there's none) with:

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement** — `lib/index/watch.ts`:
```ts
import { watch } from "chokidar";
import path from "path";
import { IGNORED_DIRS } from "./discover";
import { TYPESCRIPT_EXTENSIONS } from "./languages/typescript";
import { normalizeSlashes } from "./paths";

export type Batcher = { add(item: string): void; flush(): void; cancel(): void };

/**
 * Collects items until `quietMs` pass with nothing new, then hands them all over at once, sorted. That's the 1 s
 * debounce structure updates get (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6). A steady trickle, like a formatter walking the tree, can't hold
 * a batch back forever: `maxWaitMs` after a batch's first item it goes anyway.
 */
export function createBatcher(onFlush: (items: string[]) => void, timing: { quietMs: number; maxWaitMs: number }): Batcher {
    let items = new Set<string>();
    let quiet: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
    let deadline: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

    const clearTimers = (): void => {
        if (quiet) clearTimeout(quiet);
        if (deadline) clearTimeout(deadline);
        quiet = null;
        deadline = null;
    };

    const flush = (): void => {
        clearTimers();
        if (items.size === 0) return;
        const batch = [...items].sort();
        items = new Set();
        onFlush(batch);
    };

    return {
        add(item){
            items.add(item);
            if (quiet) clearTimeout(quiet);
            quiet = setTimeout(flush, timing.quietMs);
            deadline ??= setTimeout(flush, timing.maxWaitMs);
        },
        flush,
        cancel(){
            clearTimers();
            items = new Set();
        },
    };
}

/** What a watcher reports: source files, plus the files that change which sources exist or what they resolve to. */
export function isWatchedFile(rel: string): boolean {
    const base = rel.slice(rel.lastIndexOf("/") + 1);
    return TYPESCRIPT_EXTENSIONS.some((ext) => rel.endsWith(ext)) || /^tsconfig[^/]*\.json$/.test(base) || base === ".gitignore";
}

export type RepoWatcher = { ready: Promise<void>; close(): Promise<void> };

/**
 * Watches a repo for changes the index cares about (PIVOT_SPEC.md §5.6) and reports them in batches, as repo-relative
 * paths. It only says "look at these": the incremental update (update.ts) decides what each path now means. A deleted
 * folder is reported as the folder's own path, and the update expands it. Folders the indexer never enters
 * (node_modules, .git…) are never watched: node_modules alone can hold more files than the repo.
 */
export function watchRepository(repoRoot: string, onChanges: (paths: string[]) => void, options: { quietMs?: number; maxWaitMs?: number } = {}): RepoWatcher {
    const root = path.resolve(repoRoot);
    const batcher = createBatcher(onChanges, { quietMs: options.quietMs ?? 1000, maxWaitMs: options.maxWaitMs ?? 5000 });
    const relative = (absolute: string): string => normalizeSlashes(path.relative(root, absolute));

    const watcher = watch(root, {
        ignoreInitial: true,
        ignored: (absolute, stats) => {
            const rel = relative(absolute);
            if (rel === "") return false;
            if (rel.split("/").some((segment) => IGNORED_DIRS.has(segment))) return true;
            // chokidar asks again with stats once it has them; until then a dotted folder name can't be told from a file
            return stats?.isFile() === true && !isWatchedFile(rel);
        },
    });

    watcher.on("all", (event, absolute) => {
        if (event === "addDir") return;   // the files inside report themselves
        const rel = relative(absolute);
        if (event === "unlinkDir" || isWatchedFile(rel)) batcher.add(rel);
    });

    const ready = new Promise<void>((resolve) => watcher.once("ready", () => resolve()));
    return {
        ready,
        async close(){
            batcher.cancel();
            await watcher.close();
        },
    };
}
```
