### Task 23: S1's margin

S1 asks for ≤ 60 s for the structure stage. Index Benchmark #2 measured 56.4 s cold, and that session's parse step ran about 5 s slower than #1's on unchanged code, so machine variance alone could cross the line.

**Part A** measures it three times. **Part B**, only if the rule says so, moves the code-search fill off the structure stage. The code search triggers (ADR-031) took the write step from 13.1 s to 27.4 s, while one bulk `INSERT … SELECT` fills the same index in about 5 s (PIVOT_SPEC.md §1).

#### Part A — Measure

- [ ] **Step 1: Three cold runs.** Nothing else heavy may run; stop the preview. For each of three runs:
  1. Make a throwaway home that holds only the migrations. `<scratchpad>` is your session's scratchpad directory, written with forward slashes:
```bash
H="<scratchpad>/neb-s1-home"; rm -rf "$H"; mkdir -p "$H/lib/db"; cp -r lib/db/migrations "$H/lib/db/migrations"
```
  2. Run `NEBULOSITY_HOME="$H" npm run index -- ../vscode` and record the five step timings and the total. This doesn't touch the owner's database or config.
- [ ] **Step 2: Decide.**
  - If **all three totals are ≤ 54 s** (10% headroom), S1 holds with margin. Record the runs as Index Benchmark #3 (format below, without the Part B row), commit, push, and **skip Part B**.
  - Otherwise do Part B.

- [ ] **Step 4: The migration.** Run `npx drizzle-kit generate --custom --name v1_3_deferred_code_search` and fill the file with exactly this:
```sql
-- A bulk structure run can defer code search (ADR-031, amended): while this meta row exists the chunk trigger
-- indexes nothing, and one INSERT … SELECT fills code_fts after the structure stage, about three times faster
-- than a trigger per chunk. The delete trigger is unchanged.
DROP TRIGGER `chunks_fts_insert`;
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TRIGGER `chunks_fts_insert` AFTER INSERT ON `chunks`
WHEN NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM `meta` WHERE `key` = 'codeSearchDeferred')
BEGIN
  INSERT INTO `code_fts`(rowid, path, name, name_split, text)
  VALUES (new.id, (SELECT path FROM files WHERE id = new.file_id), new.name, new.name_split, new.text);
END;
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The store** — append to `lib/db/store/search.ts`, adding `meta` to the schema import:
```ts

/** The meta row that stops the chunk trigger while a bulk run writes (migration 0006, ADR-031 amended). */
const DEFERRED_KEY = "codeSearchDeferred";

/** Stops the chunk trigger from indexing code search until fillCodeSearch runs. Only a run holding the index lock calls it. */
export function deferCodeSearch(db: Db): void {
    db.insert(meta).values({ key: DEFERRED_KEY, value: new Date().toISOString() }).onConflictDoNothing().run();
}

export function isCodeSearchDeferred(db: Db): boolean {
    return db.select({ key: meta.key }).from(meta).where(eq(meta.key, DEFERRED_KEY)).get() !== undefined;
}

/**
 * Indexes every chunk code search is missing, from every repo, in one statement. Then the trigger takes over again.
 * Also the repair for a bulk run that died before getting here. Returns how many chunks it indexed.
 */
export function fillCodeSearch(db: Db): number {
    let filled = 0;
    db.transaction((tx) => {
        filled = tx.run(sql`
            insert into code_fts(rowid, path, name, name_split, text)
            select c.id, f.path, c.name, c.name_split, c.text
            from chunks c join files f on f.id = c.file_id
            where not exists (select 1 from code_fts where code_fts.rowid = c.id)`).changes;
        tx.delete(meta).where(eq(meta.key, DEFERRED_KEY)).run();
    });
    return filled;
}
```

Append the five rows below to that table, after ADR-035. The header here is only for reading; don't copy it.

    | ADR | Decision |
    |---|---|
    | ADR-036 | The Map reads one level at a time: children in size order, a 50-box fold with a `<level>/` "+N more" box, an index version for caching and refetching, area counts from an in-memory import graph |
    | ADR-037 | Incremental structure updates re-resolve only what can have changed and write only the differences, with level edges moved by ±1 per import |
    | ADR-038 | A forked background worker owns the index while the Map is open: watcher, incremental updates, summary refresh (60 s, ≤ 50 files), a folder sweep, sliced embeddings |
    | ADR-039 | The live channel: SSE out, a localhost-only POST in (Host and Origin checked), the server bound to loopback |
    | ADR-040 | Renderer-agnostic contracts (`CONTRACTS_VERSION` 2); the version names the API and migrations carry the data |

## What comes next (not part of this plan)

The V1.4 plan (MCP) is written with superpowers:writing-plans at the start of that slice. V1.3 leaves it these seams:
- **The tool registry** starts from `lib/agent/tools.ts`, ported here onto the new engine (PIVOT_SPEC.md §6.2).
- **`dependents`, `dependencies` and `path`** are BFS over `lib/engine/graph.ts`'s `ImportGraph`, which is already cached per index version.
- **Live events.** The MCP server posts them with `postLiveEvent` (`lib/live/client.ts`): the Map already pulses them, and `show_on_map` is a `LiveEvent` with an `action`.
- **Freshness.** Its notes come from `stalePaths` and `freshnessNote` (`lib/engine/freshness.ts`).
- **A read-only database.** The MCP server opens the database read-only (PIVOT_SPEC.md §3, rule 5). `getDb()` migrates on open, so V1.4 adds a read-only variant that never migrates.

These are carried forward on purpose:
- **The startup check's size-and-mtime filter** (§5.6). The worker's warm full run takes 4.9 s on VS Code; revisit if a bigger repo makes start-up slow.
- **A worker shared across servers.** If two Next servers ever run against one database, their workers would both watch, and both would serialize on the index lock. V1.3 assumes one server.
